[p13.b1] 2.2 | MILL- LIMITED WARRANTY CERTIFICATE
[p13.b2] Limited Warranty Certificate
[p13.b3] Haas Automation, Inc. Covering Haas Automation, Inc. CNC Equipment
[p13.b4] Effective September 1, 2010
[p13.b5] Limited Warranty Coverage
[p13.b6] Each CNC Machine and its Components (collectively, “Haas Products”) are warranted by Manufacturer against defects in material and workmanship. This warranty is provided only to an end-user of the CNC Machine (a “Customer”). The period of this limited warranty is one (1) year. The warranty period commences on the date the CNC Machine is installed at the Customer’s facility. Customer may purchase an extension of the warranty period from an authorized Haas distributor (a “Warranty Extension”), any time during the first year of ownership.
[p13.b7] Repair or Replacement Only
[p13.b8] Manufacturer’s sole liability, and Customer’s exclusive remedy under this warranty, with respect to any and all Haas products, shall be limited to repairing or replacing, at the discretion of the Manufacturer, the defective Haas product.
[p13.b9] Disclaimer of Warranty
[p13.b10] This warranty is Manufacturer’s sole and exclusive warranty, and is in lieu of all other warranties of whatever kind or nature, express or implied, written or oral, including, but not limited to, any implied warranty of merchantability, implied warranty of fitness for a particular purpose, or other warranty of quality or performance or noninfringement. All such other warranties of whatever kind are hereby disclaimed by Manufacturer and waived by Customer.
[p13.b11] Haas Automation Inc. (“Haas” or “Manufacturer”) provides a limited warranty on all new mills, turning centers, and rotary machines (collectively, “CNC Machines”) and their components (except those listed below under Limits and Exclusions of Warranty) (“Components”) that are manufactured by Haas and sold by Haas or its authorized distributors as set forth in this Certificate. The warranty set forth in this Certificate is a limited warranty, it is the only warranty by Manufacturer, and is subject to the terms and conditions of this Certificate.
[p13.b12] Limits and Exclusions of Warranty
[p13.b13.1] Components subject to wear during normal use and over time, including, but not limited to, paint, window finish and condition, light bulbs, seals, wipers, gaskets, chip removal system (e.g., augers, chip chutes), belts, filters, door rollers, tool changer fingers, etc., are excluded from this warranty. Manufacturer’s specified maintenance procedures must be adhered to and recorded in order to maintain this warranty.
[p13.b13.2] This warranty is void if Manufacturer determines that (i) any Haas Product was subjected to mishandling, misuse, abuse, neglect, accident, improper installation, improper maintenance, improper storage, or improper operation or application, including the use of improper coolants or other fluids, (ii) any Haas Product was improperly repaired or serviced by Customer, an unauthorized service technician, or other unauthorized person, (iii) Customer or any person makes or attempts to make any modification to any Haas Product without the prior written authorization of Manufacturer, and/or (iv) any Haas Product was used for any non-commercial use (such as personal or household use).
[p13.b13.3] This warranty does not cover damage or defect due to an external influence or matters beyond the reasonable control of Manufacturer, including, but not limited to, theft, vandalism, fire, weather condition (such as rain, flood, wind, lightning, or earthquake), or acts of war or terrorism.
[p13.b14] Without limiting the generality of any of the exclusions or limitations described in this Certificate, this warranty does not include any warranty that any Haas Product will meet any person’s production specifications or other requirements, or that operation of any Haas Product will be uninterrupted or error-free. Manufacturer assumes no responsibility with respect to the use of any Haas Product by any person, and Manufacturer shall not incur any liability to any person for any failure in design, production, operation, performance, or otherwise of any Haas Product, other than repair or replacement of same as set forth in the warranty above.

[p15.b1] 2.3 | CUSTOMER SATISFACTION POLICY
[p15.b2] Customer Satisfaction Policy
[p15.b3] Dear Haas Customer,
[p15.b4] Your complete satisfaction and goodwill are of the utmost importance to both Haas Automation, Inc. and the Haas distributor (HFO) where you purchased your equipment. Normally, your HFO will rapidly resolve any concerns you have about your sales transaction or the operation of your equipment.
[p15.b5] However, if your concerns are not resolved to your complete satisfaction, and you have discussed your concerns with a member of the HFO’s management, the General Manager, or the HFO’s owner directly, please do the following:
[p15.b6] Contact Haas Automation’s Customer Service Advocate at 805-988-6980. So that we may resolve your concerns as quickly as possible, please have the following information available when you call:
[p15.b7] • Your company name, address, and phone number
[p15.b8] • The machine model and serial number
[p15.b9] • The HFO name, and the name of your latest contact at the HFO
[p15.b10] • The nature of your concern
[p15.b11] If you wish to write Haas Automation, please use this address:
[p15.b12] Haas Automation, Inc. U.S.A. 2800 Sturgis Road Oxnard CA 93030 Att: Customer Satisfaction Manager email: customerservice@HaasCNC.com
[p15.b13] Once you contact the Haas Automation Customer Service Center, we will make every effort to work directly with you and your HFO to quickly resolve your concerns. At Haas Automation, we know that a good Customer-Distributor- Manufacturer relationship will help ensure continued success for all concerned.
[p15.b14] INTERNATIONAL:
[p15.b15] Haas Automation, Europe Mercuriusstraat 28, B-1930 Zaventem, Belgium email: customerservice@HaasCNC.com
[p15.b16] Haas Automation, Asia No. 96 Yi Wei Road 67, Waigaoqiao FTZ Shanghai 200131 P.R.C. email: customerservice@HaasCNC.com
[p15.b17] Customer Feedback
[p15.b18] If you have concerns or questions regarding this Operator’s Manual, please contact us on our website, www.HaasCNC. com. Use the “Contact Us” link and send your comments to the Customer Advocate.

=== page 17 ===
[p17.b1] 3.1 | MILL - SAFETY
[p17.b2] Safety Notes
[p17.b3] CAUTION: Only authorized and trained personnel may operate this equipment. You must always act in accordance with the Operator’s manual, safety decals, safety procedures, and instructions for safe machine operation. Untrained personnel present a hazard to themselves and the machine
[p17.b4] IMPORTANT: Do not operate this machine until you have read all warnings, cautions, and instructions
[p17.b5] CAUTION: The sample programs in this manual have been tested for accuracy, but they are for illustrative purposes only. The programs do not define tools, offsets, or materials. They do not describe workholding or other fixturing. If you choose to run a sample program on your machine, do so in Graphics mode. Always follow safe machining practices when you run an unfamiliar program.
[p17.b6] All CNC machines present hazards from rotating cutting tools, belts and pulleys, high voltage electricity, noise, and compressed air. When you use CNC machines and their components, you must always follow basic safety precautions to reduce the risk of personal injury and mechanical damage. The work area must be adequately illuminated to allow clear view and safe operation of the machine. This includes the operator work area and all areas of the machine that might be accessed during maintenance or cleaning. Adequate illumination is the responsibility of the user.
[p17.b7] Cutting tools, workholding, workpiece and coolant are beyond the scope and control of Haas Automation, Inc. Each of these potential hazards associated with it (sharp edges, heavy lifting considerations, chemical composition, etc) and it is the responsibility of the user to take appropriate action (PPE, training, etc).
[p17.b8] Cleaning of the machine is required during normal use and prior to maintenance or repair. Optional equipment is available to aid cleaning such as washdown hoses, chip conveyors and chip augers. Safe use of this equipment requires training and might require appropriate PPE and is the responsibility of the user.
[p17.b9] This operator’s manual is intended as a reference guide and is not to be the sole source of training. Complete operator training is available from the authorized Haas distributor.
[p17.b10] Summary of Types of Operation for Haas Automation Machine Tools
[p17.b11] Haas CNC Mills are intended for cutting and shaping of metals and other hard materials. They are general purpose in nature and a list of all of those materials and types of cutting would never be complete. Almost all cutting and shaping is performed by a rotating tool mounted in a spindle. Rotation of the mill is not required. Some cutting operations require liquid coolant. That coolant is also an option depending on the type of cutting.
[p17.b12] Operations of Haas Mills are separated into three areas. They are: Operations, Maintenance, and Service. Operations and Maintenance are intended to be performed by a trained and qualified machine operator. This Operator’s Manual contains some of the information necessary to operate the machine. All other machine operations are to be considered Service. Service is only to be performed by specially trained service personnel.
[p17.b13] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 17
=== page 18 ===
[p18.b1] 3.1 | MILL - SAFETY
[p18.b2] Operation of this machine consists of the following:
[p18.b3] (step 1) 1. Machine Setup Machine setup is done to initially set up the tools, offsets, and fixtures required to perform a repetitive function that later is called machine operation. Some machine setup functions can be done with the door open but are limited to “hold to run”.
[p18.b4] (step 2) 2. Machine operating in Automatic Mode Automatic operation is initiated with Cycle-Start and can only be done with the doors closed.
[p18.b5] (step 3) 3. Operator loading and unloading of materials (parts) Parts loading and unloading is what precedes and follows an automatic operation. This must be done with the doors open and all machine automatic motion is stopped when the door is open.
[p18.b6] (step 4) 4. Operator loading and unloading of cutting tools Tool loading and unloading is done less often than setup. It is often required when a tool has become worn and must be replaced.
[p18.b7] Maintenance only consists of the following:
[p18.b8] (step 1) 1. Adding and maintaining condition of coolant Adding coolant and maintaining coolant concentration is required at regular intervals. This is a normal operator function and is either done from a safe location outside of the work enclosure or with the doors open and the machine stopped.
[p18.b9] (step 2) 2. Adding lubricants Adding lubricants for spindle and axes is required at regular intervals. These are often months or years in length. This is a normal operator function and is always done from a safe location outside of the work enclosure.
[p18.b10] (step 3) 3. Cleaning chips out of the machine Cleaning out of chips is required at intervals dictated by the type of machining performed. This is a normal operator function. It is performed with the doors open and all of the machine operation is stopped.
[p18.b11] Service only consists of the following:
[p18.b12] (step 1) 1. Repairing of a machine that is not operating correctly Any machine that is not operating correctly requires service by factory trained personnel. This is never an operator function. It is not considered maintenance. Installation and service instructions are provided separately from the Operator’s Manual.
[p18.b13] (step 2) 2. Machine moving, unpacking, and installation Haas machines are shipped to a user’s location almost ready to operate. They still require a trained service person to complete the installation. Installation and service instructions are provided separately from the Operator’s Manual.
[p18.b14] (step 3) 3. Machine packing Machine packing for shipment requires the same packing material supplied by Haas in the original shipment. Packing requires a trained service person to complete the installation. Shipping instructions are provided separately from the Operator’s Manual.
[p18.b15] (step 4) 4. Decommission, dismantle and disposal Machine is not expected to be disassembled for shipment; it can be moved in its entirety in the same manner in which it was installed. Machine can be returned to the manufacturer’s distributor for disposal; manufacturer accepts any/all components for recycling per Directive 2002/96/EC.
[p18.b16] (step 5) 5. End-of-life disposal End-of-life disposal must conform to the laws and regulations in the region the machine is located. This is a jointly the responsibility of the owner and seller of the machine. The risk analysis does not address this phase.
[p18.b17] 18 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 19 ===
[p19.b1] 3.2 | MILL - READ BEFORE OPERATING
[p19.b2] READ BEFORE OPERATING
[p19.b3] DANGER: Do not enter the machining area any time the machine is in motion, or at any time that machine motion is possible. Severe injury or death may result. Motion is possible when the power is on and the machine is not in [EMERGENCY STOP].
[p19.b4] Basic safety: • This machine can cause severe bodily injury.
[p19.b5] • This machine is automatically controlled and may start at any time.
[p19.b6] • Consult your local safety codes and regulations before you operate the machine. Contact your dealer if you have questions about safety issues.
[p19.b7] • It is the machine owner’s responsibility to make sure that everyone who is involved in installing and operating the machine is fully acquainted with the operation and safety instructions provided with the machine, BEFORE they work with the machine. The ultimate responsibility for safety rests with the machine owner and the individuals who work with the machine.
[p19.b8] • Use appropriate eye and ear protection when you operate the machine.
[p19.b9] • Use appropriate gloves to remove processed material and to clean the machine.
[p19.b10] • Replace windows immediately if they are damaged or severely scratched.
[p19.b11] • Keep the side windows locked during operation (if available).
[p19.b12] Electrical safety: • The electrical power must meet the required specifications. Attempting to run the machine from any other source can cause severe damage and will void the warranty.
[p19.b13] • The electrical panel should be closed and the key and latches on the control cabinet should be secured at all times, except during installation and service. At those times, only qualified electricians should have access to the panel. When the main circuit breaker is on, there is high voltage throughout the electrical panel (including the circuit boards and logic circuits) and some components operate at high temperatures; therefore, extreme caution is required. Once the machine is installed, the control cabinet must be locked, with the key available only to qualified service personnel.
[p19.b14] • Do not reset a circuit breaker until the reason for the fault is investigated and understood. Only Haas-trained service personnel should troubleshoot and repair Haas equipment.
[p19.b15] • Do not press [POWER UP] on the control pendant before the machine is fully installed.
[p19.b16] Operation Safety: DANGER: To avoid injury verify that the spindle has stopped turning before opening the doors. In the event of a loss of power the spindle will take much longer to coast to a stop.
[p19.b17] • Do not operate the machine unless the doors are closed and the door interlocks are functioning correctly.
[p19.b18] • Check for damaged parts and tools before you operate the machine. Any part or tool that is damaged should be properly repaired or replaced by authorized personnel. Do not operate the machine if any component does not appear to be functioning correctly.
[p19.b19] • Rotating cutting tools can cause severe injury. When a program runs, the mill table and spindle head can move rapidly at any time.
[p19.b20] • Improperly clamped parts machined at high speeds/feeds may be ejected and puncture the enclosure. It is not safe to machine oversized or marginally clamped parts. CAUTION: Manual or Automatic closing of the enclosure doors is a potential pinch point. With Auto Door, the door may be programmed to close automatically, or by pressing the door open/close button on the operators pendant. Avoid putting hands or appendages in the door while closing either manually or automatically.
[p19.b21] Release of person trapped in the machine: • No person should ever be located inside the machine during operation.
[p19.b22] • In the unlikely event that a person is trapped inside the machine the emergency stop button should be immediately be depressed and the person removed.
[p19.b23] • If the person is pinched or entangled the machine should be powered off; then the machine axes can be moved by use of a large external force in the direction required to free the person.
[p19.b24] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 19
=== page 20 ===
[p20.b1] 3.2 | MILL - READ BEFORE OPERATING
[p20.b2] Recover from a jam or blockage: • Of the chip conveyor - Follow the cleaning instructions on the Haas service site (go to www.haascnc.com click on the Service tab). If necessary, close the doors and reverse the conveyor so the jammed part or material is accessible, and remove.
[p20.b3] • Use lifting equipment or get assistance for lifting heavy and awkward parts.
[p20.b4] • Of a tool and material/part - Close the doors, press [RESET] to clear and displayed alarms. Jog the axis so the tool and material are clear.
[p20.b5] • Of the Automatic Tool Changer/tool and spindle - Press [RECOVER] and follow the on-screen instructions.
[p20.b6] • If the alarms do not reset or you are unable to clear a blockage, contact your Haas Factory Outlet (HFO) for assistance. Follow these guidelines when you work with the machine: • Normal operation - Keep the door closed and guards in place (for non-enclosed machines) while the machine operates.
[p20.b7] • Part loading and unloading – An operator opens the door, completes the task, closes the door, and then presses [CYCLE START] (starting automatic motion).
[p20.b8] • Machining job set-up – When set-up is complete, turn the set-up key to lock out set-mode and remove the key.
[p20.b9] • Maintenance / Machine Cleaner– Press [EMERGENCY STOP] or [POWER OFF] on the machine before you enter the enclosure.
[p20.b10] Periodic inspection of machine safety features: • Inspect door interlock mechanism for proper fit and function.
[p20.b11] • Inspect safety windows and enclosure for damage or leaks.
[p20.b12] • Verify all enclosure panels are in place.
[p20.b13] Door Safety Interlock inspection: • Inspect the door interlock, verify the door interlock key is not bent, misaligned, and that all fasteners are installed.
[p20.b14] • Inspect the door interlock itself for any signs of obstruction or misalignment.
[p20.b15] • Immediately replace an components of the Door Safety Interlock system that do not meet this criteria.
[p20.b16] Door Safety Interlock verification: • With the machine in run mode, close the machine door, run the spindle at 100 RPM, pull the door and verify the door does not open.
[p20.b17] MACHINE ENCLOSURE AND SAFETY GLASS INSPECTION AND TESTING: Routine Inspection: • Visually inspect the enclosure and safety glass for any signs of distortion, breakage or other damage.
[p20.b18] Replace the Lexan windows after 7 years or if they are [p20.b19] damaged or severely scratched.
[p20.b20] • Keep all safety glass and machine windows clean to allow proper viewing of the machine during operations.
[p20.b21] • A daily visual inspection of the machine enclosure to verify all panels are in place should be performed.
[p20.b22] Testing of machine enclosure: • No testing of the machine enclosure is necessary.

[p21.b2] Machine Environmental Limits
[p21.b3] This table list the environmental limits for safe operation:

[p21.t1.r2] column 1: Operating Temperature | MINIMUM: 41 °F (5.0 °C) | MAXIMUM: 122 °F (50.0 °C)
[p21.t1.r3] column 1: Storage Temperature | MINIMUM: -4 °F (-20.0 °C) | MAXIMUM: 158 °F (70.0 °C)

[p21.b5] CAUTION: Do not operate the machine in explosive atmospheres (explosive vapors and/ or particulate matter).
[p21.b6] Machine with Haas Robot Package Machine and robot environment is intended to be a machine shop or industrial installation. Shop lighting is the users responsibility.
[p21.b7] Machine Noise Limits
[p21.b8] CAUTION: Take precautions to prevent hearing damage from machine/machining noise. Wear ear protection, change your application (tooling, spindle speed, axis speed, fixturing, programmed path) to reduce noise, or restrict access to machine area during cutting.
[p21.b9] NOTE: Actual noise levels while cutting material are greatly affected by the user’s choice of material, cutting tools, speeds and feeds, workholding and other factors. These factors are application specific and are controlled by the user, not Haas Automation Inc
[p21.b10] Typical noise levels at the operator’s position during normal operation are as follows:
[p21.b11] • A-Weighted sound pressure level measurements will be 69.4dB or lower.
[p21.b12] • C-Weighted instantaneous sound pressure levels will be 78.0dB or lower.
[p21.b13] • LwA (sound power level A-weighted) will be 75.0dB or lower.

=== page 22 ===
[p22.b1] 3.4 | MILL - UNATTENDED OPERATION
[p22.b2] Unattended Operation
[p22.b3] Fully enclosed Haas CNC machines are designed to operate unattended; however, your machining process may not be safe to operate unmonitored.
[p22.b4] As it is the shop owner’s responsibility to set up the machine safely and use best practice machining techniques, it is also the owner’s responsibility to manage the progress of these methods. You must monitor your machining process to prevent damage, injury, or loss of life if a hazardous condition occurs.
[p22.b5] For example, if there is the risk of fire due to the material machined, then you must install an appropriate fire suppression system to reduce the risk of harm to personnel, equipment, and the building. Contact a specialist to install monitoring tools before machines are allowed to run unattended.
[p22.b6] It is especially important to select monitoring equipment that can immediately detect a problem and perform an appropriate action without human intervention.
[p22.b7] 22 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 23 ===
[p23.b1] 3.5 | MILL - DOOR RULES
[p23.b2] Run/Setup Mode Restrictions
[p23.b3] All Haas CNC machines are equipped with locks on the operator doors and a key switch on the side of the control pendant to lock and unlock setup mode. Generally, setup mode status (locked or unlocked) affects how the machine operates when the doors are opened.
[p23.b4] Setup mode should be locked out (the keyswitch in the vertical, locked position) at most times. In Run and in Setup mode, the enclosure doors are locked closed during CNC program execution, spindle rotation or axis movement. The doors automatically unlock when the machine is not in cycle. Many machine functions are unavailable with the door open.
[p23.b5] When unlocked, setup mode allows a skilled machinist more access to the machine to set up jobs. In this mode, machine behavior is dependent on whether the doors are opened or closed. The following charts summarize the modes and allowed functions.
[p23.b6] Note: All these conditions follow assuming that the door is open and stays open before, during and the actions occur.
[p23.b7] Danger: Do not attempt to override safety features. Doing so makes the machine unsafe and voids the warranty.

=== page 24 ===
[p24.b1] 3.6 | MILL - ROBOT CELLS
[p24.b2] ROBOT CELLS
[p24.b3] A machine in a robot cell is allowed to run a program while the door is open, regardless of the position of the Run-Setup key. While the door is open, the spindle speed is limited to the lower of the factory RPM limit or Setting 292, Door Open Spindle Speed Limit. If the door is opened while the spindle RPM is above the limit, the spindle will decelerate to the limit RPM. Closing the door removes the limit and the programmed RPM is restored.
[p24.b4] This open-door condition is allowed only while a robot communicates with the CNC machine. Typically, an interface between the robot and the CNC machine addresses the safety of both machines.
[p24.b5] 24 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 25 ===
[p25.b1] 3.7 | MILL - MIST EXTRACTION/ENCLOSURE EVACUATION
[p25.b2] Mist Extraction / Enclosure Evacuation
[p25.b3] Some models have a provision installed that will allow for a mist extractor to be attached to the machine.
[p25.b4] There is also an optional enclosure exhaust system available that helps keep the mist out of the machine enclosure. It is entirely up to the owner/operator to determine if and what type of mist extractor is best suited for the application.
[p25.b5] The owner/operator assumes all responsibility for the installation of the mist extraction system.
[p25.b6] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 25

[p26.b4] This feature will display a warning message when the [FWD] or [REV] button is pressed and the previous commanded spindle speed is above the Spindle Maximum Manual Speed parameter. Press [ENTER] to go to the previous commanded spindle speed or press [CANCEL] to cancel the action.

[p27.b1] 3.9 | MILL - MODIFICATION TO THE MACHINE
[p27.b2] Modifications to the Machine
[p27.b3] Haas Automation, Inc. is not responsible for damage caused by modifications you make to your Haas machine(s) with parts or kits not manufactured or sold by Haas Automation, Inc. The use of such parts or kits may void your warranty.
[p27.b4] Some parts or kits manufactured or sold by Haas Automation, Inc. are considered user-installable. If you choose to install these parts or kits yourself, be sure to completely read the accompanying installation instructions. Make sure you understand the procedure, and how to do it safely, before you begin. If you have any doubts about your ability to complete the procedure, contact your Haas Factory Outlet (HFO) for assistance.

=== page 28 ===
[p28.b1] 3.10 | MILL - IMPROPER COOLANTS
[p28.b2] Improper Coolants
[p28.b3] Coolant is an important part of many machining operations. When it is correctly used and maintained, coolant can improve part finish, lengthen tool life, and protect machine components from rust and other damage. Improper coolants, however, can cause significant damage to your machine.
[p28.b4] Machine coolant must be water-soluble, synthetic oil-based or synthetic-based coolant or lubricant. NOTE: Be sure to maintain your coolant mixture to keep the coolant concentrate at acceptable levels. Improperly maintained coolant mixtures can allow machine components to rust. Rust damage is not covered by your warranty. Ask your HFO or your coolant dealer if you have questions about the specific coolant that you plan to use.
[p28.b5] Such damage can void your warranty, but it can also introduce hazardous conditions to your shop. For example, coolant leaks through damaged seals could create a slipping hazard.
[p28.b6] Improper coolant use includes, but is not limited to, these points:
[p28.b7] • Do not use plain water. This causes machine components to rust.
[p28.b8] • Do not use flammable coolants.
[p28.b9] • Do not use straight or “neat” mineral-oil products. These products cause damage to rubber seals and tubing throughout the machine. If you use a minimum- quantity lubrication system for near-dry machining, use only the recommended oils.
[p28.b10] 28 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 29 ===
[p29.b1] 3.11 | MILL - SAFETY DECALS
[p29.b2] Safety Decals
[p29.b3] The Haas factory puts decals on your machine to quickly communicate possible hazards. If decals become damaged or worn, or if you need additional decals to emphasize a particular safety point, contact your Haas Factory Outlet (HFO).
[p29.b4] NOTE: Never alter or remove any safety decal or symbol.
[p29.b5] Be sure to familiarize yourself with the symbols on the safety decals. The symbols are designed to quickly tell you the type of information they give:
[p29.b6] • Yellow Triangle - Describes a hazard.
[p29.b7] • Red Circle with Slash-Through - Describes a prohibited action.
[p29.b8] • Green Circle - Describes a recommended action.
[p29.b9] • Black Circle - Gives information about machine or accessory operation.
[p29.b10] Example Safety Decal Symbols: [1] Hazard Description, [2] Prohibited Action, [3] Recommended Action.
[p29.b11] You may find other decals on your machine, depending on the model and options installed. Be sure to read and understand these decals.
[p29.b12] 1
[p29.b13] 2
[p29.b14] 3
[p29.b15] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 29
=== page 30 ===
[p30.b1] 3.11 | MILL - SAFETY DECALS
[p30.b2] Hazard Symbols - Yellow Triangles Prohibited Action Symbols - Red Circles with Slash-Through
[p30.b3] Moving parts can entangle, trap, crush, and cut. Keep all parts of your body away from machine parts when they move, or whenever motion is possible. Motion is possible when the power is on and the machine is not in [EMERGENCY STOP]. Secure loose clothing, hair, etc. Remember that automatically controlled devices can start at any time.
[p30.b4] Do not touch rotating tools. Keep all parts of your body away from machines parts when they move, or whenever motion is possible. Motion is possible when the power is on and the machine is not in [EMERGENCY STOP]. Sharp tools and chips can easily cut skin.
[p30.b5] The Regen is used by the spindle drive to dissipate excess power and will get hot. Always use care around the Regen.
[p30.b6] There are high voltage components on the machine that can cause electrical shock. Always use care around high voltage components.
[p30.b7] There are high voltage components on the machine that can cause arc flash, and electrical shock. Take care to avoid opening electrical enclosures unless components are de-energized or proper personal protective equipment is worn. Arc flash ratings are on the nameplate.
[p30.b8] Long tools are dangerous, especially at spindle speeds higher than 5000 RPM. The tools can break and eject from the machine. Remember that machine enclosures are intended to stop coolant and chips. Enclosures may not stop broken tools or thrown parts. Always check your setup and tooling before you start machining.
[p30.b9] Machining operations can create hazardous chips, dust, or mist. This is a function of the materials being cut, the metalworking fluid and cutting tools used, and the machining speeds/feeds.
[p30.b10] It is up to the owner/operator of the machine to determine if personal protective equipment such as safety goggles or a respirator is required and also if a mist extraction system is needed.
[p30.b11] Some models have a provision for connecting a mist extraction system. Always read and understand the Safety Data Sheets (SDS) for the workpiece material, the cutting tools, and the metalworking fluid.
[p30.b12] Other Safety Information
[p30.b13] Do not enter the machine enclosure when the machine is capable of automatic motion. When you must enter the enclosure to complete tasks, press [EMERGENCY STOP] or power off the machine. Put a safety tag on the control pendant to alert other people that you are inside the machine, and that they must not turn on or operate the machine.
[p30.b14] Do not machine ceramics.
[p30.b15] Do not attempt to load tools with the spindle dogs misaligned with the cutouts in the toolholder V-Flange.
[p30.b16] Do not machine flammable materials. Do not use flammable coolants. Flammable materials in particulate or vapor form can become explosive. The machine enclosures is not designed to contain explosions or extinguish fire.
[p30.b17] Do not use pure water as coolant. This will cause machine components to rust. Always use a rust-inhibitive coolant concentrate with water.
[p30.b18] Prohibited Action Symbols - Red Circles with Slash-Through
[p30.b19] Keep the machine doors closed.
[p30.b20] Always wear safety glasses or goggles when you are near a machine. Airborne debris can cause eye damage. Always wear hearing protection when you are near a machine. Machine noise can exceed 70 dBA.
[p30.b21] Make sure the spindle dogs are correctly aligned with the cutouts in the toolholder V-flange.
[p30.b22] Note the location of the tool release button. Press this button only when you are holding the tool. Some tools are very heavy. Handle these tools carefully; use both hands and have someone press the tool release button for you.
[p30.b23] Informational Symbols – Black Circles
[p30.b24] Maintain the recommended coolant concentration. A “lean” coolant mixture (less concentrated than recommended) may not effectively prevent machine components from rusting. A “rich” coolant mixture (more concentrated than recommended) wastes coolant concentrate without further benefit over the recommended concentration.
[p30.b25] IMPORTANT: You may find other decals on your machine, depending on the model and options installed. Be sure to read and understand these decals.
[p30.b26] 30 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual

[p31.b1] 4.1 | MILL - CONTROL PENDANT
[p31.b2] Control Pendant Overview
[p31.b3] The control pendant is the main interface to your Haas machine. This is where you program and run your CNC machining projects. This control pendant orientation section describes the different pendant sections:
[p31.b4] • Pendant front panel • Pendant right side, top, and bottom • Keyboard • Function/ Cursor Keys • Display / Mode Keys • Numeric / Alpha Keys • Jog / Override Keys
[p31.b5] 4.2 | PENDANT FRONT PANEL
[p31.b6] Front Panel Controls
[p31.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: NAME | IMAGE | FUNCTION
[p31.t1.r2] NAME: POWER ON | IMAGE:  | FUNCTION: Powers the machine on.
[p31.t1.r3] NAME: POWER OFF | IMAGE:  | FUNCTION: Powers the machine off.
[p31.t1.r4] NAME: EMERGENCY STOP | IMAGE:  | FUNCTION: Press to stop all axis motion, disable servos, stop the spindle and tool changer, and turn off the coolant pump.
[p31.t1.r5] NAME: HANDLE JOG | IMAGE:  | FUNCTION: This is used to jog axes (select in HANDLE JOG Mode). Also used to scroll through program code or menu items while editing.
[p31.t1.r6] NAME: CYCLE START | IMAGE:  | FUNCTION: Starts a program. This button is also used to start a program simulation in graphics mode.
[p31.t1.r7] NAME: FEED HOLD | IMAGE:  | FUNCTION: Stops all axis motion during a program. The spindle continues to run. Press CYCLE START to cancel.
[p31.b7] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 31

[p32.b1] 4.2 | MILL - PENDANT FRONT PANEL
[p32.b2] Pendant Right Side, and Top Panels
[p32.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: NAME | IMAGE | FUNCTION
[p32.t1.r2] NAME: USB | IMAGE:  | FUNCTION: Plug compatible USB devices into this port. It has a removable dust cap.
[p32.t1.r3] NAME: MEMORY LOCK | IMAGE:  | FUNCTION: In the locked position, this keyswitch prevents alterations to programs, settings, parameters, and offsets.
[p32.t1.r4] NAME: SETUP MODE | IMAGE:  | FUNCTION: In the locked position, this keyswitch enables all machine safety features. Unlock allows setup (refer to “Setup Mode” in the Safety section of this manual for details).
[p32.t1.r5] NAME: SECOND HOME | IMAGE:  | FUNCTION: Press to rapid all axes to the coordinates specified in settings 268 - 270. (Refer to “Settings 268 - 270” in the Settings section of this manual for details).
[p32.t1.r6] NAME: AUTO DOOR OVERRIDE | IMAGE:  | FUNCTION: Press this button to open or close the Auto Door (if equipped).
[p32.t1.r7] NAME: WORKLIGHT | IMAGE:  | FUNCTION: These buttons toggle the internal worklight and High Intensity Lighting (if equipped).
[p32.b3] Pendant Top Panel
[p32.b4] BEACON LIGHT
[p32.b5] Provides quick visual confirmation of the machine’s current status. There are five different beacon states:
[p32.t2.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: Light Status | Meaning
[p32.t2.r2] Light Status: Off | Meaning: The machine is idle.
[p32.t2.r3] Light Status: Solid Green | Meaning: The machine is running.
[p32.t2.r4] Light Status: Flashing Green | Meaning: The machine is stopped but is in a ready state. Operator input is required to continue.
[p32.t2.r5] Light Status: Flashing Red | Meaning: A fault has occurred, or the machine is in Emergency Stop
[p32.b6] 32 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual

[p33.b17] 4
[p33.b18] 7
[p33.b19] 6

[p33.b21] Do these steps to enter special symbols:
[p33.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: NAME | SYMBOL
[p33.t1.r2] NAME: _ | SYMBOL: underscore
[p33.t1.r3] NAME: ^ | SYMBOL: caret
[p33.t1.r4] NAME: ~ | SYMBOL: tilde
[p33.t1.r5] NAME: { | SYMBOL: open curly brackets
[p33.t1.r6] NAME: } | SYMBOL: closed curly brackets
[p33.t1.r7] NAME: \ | SYMBOL: backslash
[p33.t1.r8] NAME: pipe
[p33.t1.r9] NAME: < | SYMBOL: less than
[p33.t1.r10] NAME: > | SYMBOL: greater than
[p33.b22] (step 1) 1. Press LIST PROGRAMS and select a storage device. 2. Press F3. 3. Select Special Symbols and press ENTER. 4. Enter a number to copy the associated symbol to the INPUT: bar.
[p33.b23] Some special symbols are not on the keypad.
[p33.b24] For example, to change a directory’s name to
[p33.b25] MY_DIRECTORY:
[p33.b26] (step 1) 1. Highlight the directory with the name that you want to change. 2. Type MY. 3. Press F3. 4. Select SPECIAL SYMBOLS and press ENTER. 5. Press 1. 6. Type DIRECTORY. 7. Press F3. 8. Select RENAME and press ENTER.

[p34.b1] 4.4 | MILL - FUNCTION / CURSOR KEYS
[p34.b2] Function Keys
[p34.b3] List of Function Keys and How They Operate
[p34.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: NAME | KEY | FUNCTION
[p34.t1.r2] NAME: Reset | KEY: RESET | FUNCTION: Clears alarms. Clears input text. Sets overrides to default values if Setting 88 is ON.
[p34.t1.r3] NAME: Power up | KEY: POWER UP | FUNCTION: Zero returns all axes and initializes the machine control.
[p34.t1.r4] NAME: Recover | KEY: RECOVER | FUNCTION: Enters tool changer recovery mode.
[p34.t1.r5] NAME: F1- F4 | KEY: F1- F4 | FUNCTION: These buttons have different functions depending on the tab that is active.
[p34.t1.r6] NAME: Tool Offset Measure | KEY: TOOL OFFSET MEASURE | FUNCTION: Records tool length offsets during part setup.
[p34.t1.r7] NAME: Next Tool | KEY: NEXT TOOL | FUNCTION: Selects the next tool from the tool changer.
[p34.t1.r8] NAME: Tool Release | KEY: TOOL RELEASE | FUNCTION: Releases the tool from the spindle when in MDI, ZERO RETURN, or HAND JOG mode.
[p34.t1.r9] NAME: Part Zero Set | KEY: PART ZERO SET | FUNCTION: Records work coordinate offsets during part setup.
[p34.b4] FUNCTION NAME KEY
[p34.b5] Cursor Keys
[p34.b6] The cursor keys let you move between data fields, scroll through programs, and navigate through tabbed menus.
[p34.t2.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: NAME | KEY | FUNCTION
[p34.t2.r2] NAME: Home | KEY: HOME | FUNCTION: Moves the cursor to the top-most item on the screen; in editing, this is the top left block of the program.
[p34.t2.r3] NAME: Cursor Arrows | KEY: CURSOR ARROWS | FUNCTION: Moves one item, block, or field in the associated direction. The keys depict ar­ rows, but this manual refers to these keys by their spelled-out names.
[p34.t2.r4] NAME: Page Up, Page Down | KEY: PAGE UP, PAGE DOWN | FUNCTION: Used to change displays or move up/down one page when viewing a program.
[p34.t2.r5] NAME: End | KEY: END | FUNCTION: Moves the cursor to the bottom-most item on the screen. In editing, this is the last block of the program.
[p34.b7] FUNCTION NAME KEY

[p35.b3] You use the Display keys to see the machine displays, operational information, and help pages.
[p35.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: NAME | KEY | FUNCTION
[p35.t1.r2] NAME: Program | KEY: PROGRAM | FUNCTION: Selects the active program pane in most modes.
[p35.t1.r3] NAME: Position | KEY: POSITION | FUNCTION: Selects the positions display.

[p35.t1.r5] NAME: Current Commands | KEY: CURRENT COMMANDS | FUNCTION: Displays menus for Devices, Timers, Macros, Active Codes, Calculators, Advanced Tool Management (ATM), Tool Table, and Media.

[p35.t1.r7] NAME: Diagnostics | KEY: DIAGNOSTIC | FUNCTION: Displays tabs for Features, Compensation, Diagnostics, and Maintenance.
[p35.t1.r8] NAME: Settings | KEY: SETTING | FUNCTION: Displays and allows changing of user settings.
[p35.t1.r9] NAME: Help | KEY: HELP | FUNCTION: Displays help information.

=== page 36 ===
[p36.b1] 4.5 | MILL - DISPLAY / MODE KEYS
[p36.b2] Mode Keys
[p36.b3] Mode keys change the operational state of the machine. Each mode key is arrow shaped and points to the row of keys that perform functions related to that mode key. The current mode is always displayed in the top left of the screen, in Mode:Key display form.
[p36.b4] NOTE: EDIT and LIST PROGRAM can also act as display keys, where you can access program editors and the device manager without changing the machine mode. For example, while the machine runs a program, you can use the device manager (LIST PROGRAM) or background editor (EDIT) without stopping the program.
[p36.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: NAME | KEY | FUNCTION
[p36.b5] EDIT MODE KEYS
[p36.t2.r1] column 1: Edit | column 2: EDIT | column 3: Lets you edit programs in the editor. You can access the Visual Programming System (VPS) from the EDIT tabbed menu.
[p36.t2.r2] column 1: Insert | column 2: INSERT | column 3: Enters text from the input line or the clipboard into the program at the cursor position.
[p36.t2.r3] column 1: Alter | column 2: ALTER | column 3: Replaces the highlighted command or text with text from the input line or the clipboard. NOTE: ALTER does not work for offsets.
[p36.t2.r4] column 1: Delete | column 2: DELETE | column 3: Deletes the item that the cursor is on, or deletes a selected program block.
[p36.t2.r5] column 1: Undo | column 2: UNDO | column 3: Undoes up to the last 40 edit changes, and deselects a highlighted block. NOTE: UNDO does not work for deleted highlighted blocks or to recover a deleted program.
[p36.b6] MEMORY MODE KEYS
[p36.t3.r1] column 1: Memory | column 2: MEMORY | MEMORY MODE KEYS: Selects memory mode. You run programs in this mode, and the other keys in the MEM row control the ways in which the program is run. Shows OPERATION:MEM in upper left display.
[p36.t3.r2] column 1: Single Block | column 2: SINGLE BLOCK | MEMORY MODE KEYS: Toggles single block on or off. When single block is on, the control runs only one program block each time you press CYCLE START.
[p36.t3.r3] column 1: Graphics | column 2: GRAPHICS | MEMORY MODE KEYS: Opens Graphics mode.
[p36.t3.r4] column 1: Option Stop | column 2: OPTION STOP | MEMORY MODE KEYS: Toggles optional stop on or off. When optional stop is on, the machine stops when it reaches M01 commands.
[p36.t3.r5] column 1: Block Delete | column 2: BLOCK DELETE | MEMORY MODE KEYS: Toggles Block Delete On or Off. When Block Delete is On, the control ignores (does not execute) the code following a Forward Slash (/), on that same line.
[p36.b7] 36 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 37 ===
[p37.b1] 4.5 | MILL - DISPLAY / MODE KEYS
[p37.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: NAME | KEY | FUNCTION
[p37.b2] MDI MODE KEYS
[p37.t2.r1] column 1: Manual Data Input | column 2: MDI | MDI MODE KEYS: In MDI mode, you run unsaved programs or blocks of code entered from the control. Shows EDIT:MDI in upper left display.
[p37.t2.r2] column 1: Coolant | column 2: COOLANT | MDI MODE KEYS: Turns the optional coolant on and off. Also, SHIFT + COOLANT turns on and off the optional Auto Air Gun / Minimum Quantity Lubrication functions
[p37.t2.r3] column 1: Handle Scroll | column 2: HANDLE SCROLL | MDI MODE KEYS: Toggles Handle Scroll mode. This lets you use the jog handle to move the cursor in menus while the control is in jog mode.
[p37.t2.r4] column 1: Automatic Tool Changer Forward | column 2: ATC FWD | MDI MODE KEYS: Rotates the tool carousel to the next tool.
[p37.t2.r5] column 1: Automatic Tool Changer Reverse | column 2: ATC REV | MDI MODE KEYS: Rotates the tool carousel to the previous tool.
[p37.b3] HANDLE JOG MODE KEYS
[p37.t3.r1] column 1: Handle Jog | column 2: HANDLE JOG | HANDLE JOG MODE KEYS: Enters Jog mode.
[p37.t3.r2] column 1: .0001/.1 .001/1 .01/10 .1/100 | column 2: .0001 /.1, .001 / 1., .01 / 10., .1 / 100. | HANDLE JOG MODE KEYS: Selects the increment for each click of the jog handle. When the mill is in MM mode the first number is multiplied by ten when jogging the axis (e.g., .0001 becomes 0.001 mm). The bottom number sets speed after you press JOG LOCK and an axis jog key or you press and hold an axis jog key. Shows SETUP:JOG in the upper left of the display.
[p37.b4] ZERO RETURN MODE KEYS
[p37.t4.r1] column 1: Zero Return | column 2: ZERO RETURN | ZERO RETURN MODE KEYS: Selects Zero Return mode, which displays axis location in four different categories: Oper­ ator, Work G54, Machine, and Dist (distance) To Go. Select the tab to switch between the categories. Shows SETUP:ZERO in the upper-left display.
[p37.t4.r2] column 1: All | column 2: ALL | ZERO RETURN MODE KEYS: Returns all axes to machine zero. This is similar to POWER UP, except a tool change does not occur.
[p37.t4.r3] column 1: Origin | column 2: ORIGIN | ZERO RETURN MODE KEYS: Sets selected values to zero.
[p37.t4.r4] column 1: Single | column 2: SINGLE | ZERO RETURN MODE KEYS: Returns one axis to machine zero. Press the desired axis letter on the Alpha keyboard and then press SINGLE
[p37.t4.r5] column 1: Home G28 | column 2: HOME G28 | ZERO RETURN MODE KEYS: Returns all axes to zero in rapid motion. HOME G28 will also home a single axis in the same manner as SINGLE. CAUTION: Make sure the axis motion paths are clear when you press this key. There is no warning or prompt before axis motion begins.
[p37.b5] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 37

[p38.b1] 4.5 | MILL - DISPLAY / MODE KEYS
[p38.b2] Mode Keys (Cont.)
[p38.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: NAME | KEY | FUNCTION
[p38.b3] LIST PROGRAM MODE KEYS
[p38.t2.r1] column 1: List Programs | column 2: LIST PROGRAMS | LIST PROGRAM MODE KEYS: Accesses a tabbed menu to load and save programs.
[p38.t2.r2] column 1: Select Programs | column 2: SELECT PROGRAMS | LIST PROGRAM MODE KEYS: Makes the highlighted program the active program.
[p38.t2.r3] column 1: Back | column 2: BACK ARROW | LIST PROGRAM MODE KEYS: Navigates to the screen you were on before the current one. This key operates like the BACK button on a web browser.

[p39.t1.r5] NAME: Cancel | KEY: CANCEL | FUNCTION: Deletes the last character typed.

[p39.t1.r7] NAME: Enter | KEY: ENTER | FUNCTION: Answers prompts and writes input.

[p39.t1.r10] NAME: = | KEY: SHIFT, then 0 | FUNCTION: Inserts =
[p39.t1.r11] NAME: # | KEY: SHIFT, then . | FUNCTION: Inserts #

[p39.t1.r15] NAME: % | KEY: SHIFT, then 4 | FUNCTION: Inserts %
[p39.t1.r16] NAME: $ | KEY: SHIFT, then 5 | FUNCTION: Inserts $
[p39.t1.r17] NAME: ! | KEY: SHIFT, then 6 | FUNCTION: Inserts !
[p39.t1.r18] NAME: & | KEY: SHIFT, then 7 | FUNCTION: Inserts &
[p39.t1.r19] NAME: @ | KEY: SHIFT, then 8 | FUNCTION: Inserts @
[p39.t1.r20] NAME: : | KEY: SHIFT, then 9 | FUNCTION: Inserts :

=== page 40 ===
[p40.b1] 4.6 | MILL - NUMERIC / ALPHA KEYS
[p40.b2] Alpha Keys
[p40.b3] Use the alpha keys to type the letters of the alphabet, along with some special characters (printed in yellow on the main key). Press SHIFT to enter the special characters.
[p40.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: NAME | KEY | FUNCTION
[p40.t1.r2] NAME: Alphabet | KEY: A-Z | FUNCTION: Uppercase letters are the default. Press SHIFT and a letter key for lowercase.
[p40.t1.r3] NAME: End-of-block (EOB) | KEY: ; | FUNCTION: This is the end-of-block character, which signifies the end of a program line.
[p40.t1.r4] NAME: Parentheses | KEY: (, ) | FUNCTION: Separate CNC program commands from user comments. They must always be entered as a pair.
[p40.t1.r5] NAME: Shift | KEY: SHIFT | FUNCTION: Accesses additional characters on the keyboard, or shifts to lower case alpha characters. The additional characters are seen in the upper left of some of the alpha and number keys.
[p40.t1.r6] NAME: Special Characters | KEY: Press SHIFT, then an alpha key | FUNCTION: Inserts the yellow character on the upper-left of the key. These characters are used for comments, macros, and certain special features.
[p40.t1.r7] NAME: Forward Slash | KEY: SHIFT, then ; | FUNCTION: Inserts /
[p40.t1.r8] NAME: Left Bracket | KEY: SHIFT, then ( | FUNCTION: Inserts [
[p40.t1.r9] NAME: Right Bracket | KEY: SHIFT, then ) | FUNCTION: Inserts ]
[p40.b4] FUNCTION NAME KEY

=== page 41 ===
[p41.b1] 4.7 | MILL - JOG / OVERRIDE KEYS
[p41.b2] Jog Keys
[p41.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: NAME | KEY | FUNCTION
[p41.t1.r2] NAME: Chip Auger Forward | KEY: CHIP FWD | FUNCTION: Starts the chip removal system in the forward direction (out of the machine).
[p41.t1.r3] NAME: Chip Auger Stop | KEY: CHIP STOP | FUNCTION: Stops the chip removal system.
[p41.t1.r4] NAME: Chip Auger Reverse | KEY: CHIP REV | FUNCTION: Starts the chip removal system in the "reverse" direction.
[p41.t1.r5] NAME: Axis Jog Keys | KEY: +X/-X, +Y/-Y, +Z/-Z, +A/C/-A/C and +B/- B (SHIFT +A/C/-A/C) | FUNCTION: Jog axes manually. Press and hold the axis button, or press and release to select an axis and then use the jog handle.
[p41.t1.r6] NAME: Jog Lock | KEY: JOG LOCK | FUNCTION: Works with the axis jog keys. Press JOG LOCK, then an axis button, and the axis moves until you press JOG LOCK again.
[p41.t1.r7] NAME: Coolant Up | KEY: CLNT UP | FUNCTION: Moves the optional Programmable Coolant (P-Cool) nozzle up.
[p41.t1.r8] NAME: Coolant Down | KEY: CLNT DOWN | FUNCTION: Moves the optional P-Cool nozzle down.
[p41.t1.r9] NAME: Auxiliary Coolant | KEY: AUX CLNT | FUNCTION: Press this key in MDI mode to toggle the Through-Spindle Coolant (TSC) system operation, if equipped. Press SHIFT + AUX CLNT to toggle the Through Tool Air Blast (TAB) function, if equipped. Both functions also work in Run-Stop-Jog-Continue mode.
[p41.b3] FUNCTION NAME KEY

=== page 42 ===
[p42.b1] 4.7 | MILL - JOG / OVERRIDE KEYS
[p42.b2] Override Keys
[p42.b3] Overrides let you temporarily adjust the speeds and feeds in your program. For example, you can slow down rapids while you prove out a program, or adjust the feedrate to experiment with its effect on part finish, etc.
[p42.b4] You can use Settings 19, 20, and 21 to disable the feedrate, spindle, and rapid overrides, respectively.
[p42.b5] FEED HOLD acts as an override that stops rapid and feed moves when you press it. FEED HOLD also stops tool changes and part timers, but not tapping cycles or dwell timers.
[p42.b6] Press CYCLE START to continue after a FEED HOLD. When the Setup Mode key is unlocked, the door switch on the enclosure also has a similar result but displays Door Hold [p42.b7] when the door is opened. When the door is closed, the control is in Feed Hold and CYCLE START must be pressed to continue. Door Hold and FEED HOLD do not stop any auxiliary axes.
[p42.b8] You can override the standard coolant setting by pressing COOLANT. The coolant pump remains either on or off until the next M-code or operator action (see Setting 32).
[p42.b9] Use Settings 83, 87, and 88 to have M30 and M06 commands, or RESET, respectively, change overridden values back to their defaults.
[p42.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: NAME | KEY | FUNCTION
[p42.t1.r2] NAME: -10% Feedrate | KEY: -10% FEEDRATE | FUNCTION: Decreases the current feedrate by 10%.
[p42.t1.r3] NAME: 100% Feedrate | KEY: 100% FEEDRATE | FUNCTION: Sets an overridden feedrate back to the programmed feed rate.
[p42.t1.r4] NAME: +10% Feedrate | KEY: +10% FEEDRATE | FUNCTION: Increases the current feedrate by 10%.
[p42.t1.r5] NAME: Handle Control Feed Rate | KEY: HANDLE FEED | FUNCTION: Lets you use the jog handle to adjust the feedrate in 1% increments.
[p42.t1.r6] NAME: -10% Spindle | KEY: -10% SPINDLE | FUNCTION: Decreases the current spindle speed by 10%
[p42.t1.r7] NAME: 100% Spindle | KEY: 100% SPINDLE | FUNCTION: Sets the overridden spindle speed back to the programmed speed.
[p42.t1.r8] NAME: +10% Spindle | KEY: +10% SPINDLE | FUNCTION: Increases the current spindle speed by 10%.
[p42.t1.r9] NAME: Handle Spindle | KEY: HANDLE SPINDLE | FUNCTION: Lets you use the jog handle to adjust the spindle speed in 1% increments.
[p42.t1.r10] NAME: Forward | KEY: FWD | FUNCTION: Starts the spindle in the clockwise direction.
[p42.t1.r11] NAME: Stop | KEY: STOP | FUNCTION: Stops the spindle.
[p42.t1.r12] NAME: Reverse | KEY: REV | FUNCTION: Starts the spindle in the counterclockwise direction.
[p42.t1.r13] NAME: Rapids | KEY: 5% RAPID/ 25% RAPID/ 50% RAPID / 100% RAPID | FUNCTION: Limits machine rapids to the value on the key.
[p42.b10] FUNCTION NAME KEY

[p44.b1] 5.1 | MILL - CONTROL DISPLAY OVERVIEW
[p44.b2] Tabbed Menu Basic Navigation
[p44.b3] The Haas control uses tabbed menus for several modes and displays. Tabbed menus keep related data together in an easy-to-access format. To navigate these menus:
[p44.b4] (step 3) 3. To choose a different tab in the same tabbed menu, press the mode or display key again. NOTE: If the cursor is at the top of the menu screen, you can also press the UP cursor arrow key to select a different tab.
[p44.b5] (step 1) 1. Press a display or mode key.
[p44.b6] The first time you access a tabbed menu, the first tab (or sub-tab) is active. The highlight cursor is at the first available option in the tab.
[p44.b7] The current tab becomes inactive. 4. Use the cursor keys to highlight a tab or a sub-tab, and press the DOWN cursor arrow key to use the tab.
[p44.b8] (step 2) 2. Use the cursor keys or the HANDLE JOG control to move the highlight cursor within an active tab.
[p44.b9] NOTE: You cannot make the tabs active in the POSITIONS tabbed display.
[p44.b10] (step 5) 5. Press a different display or mode key to work with a different tabbed menu.
[p44.b11] Input Bar
[p44.b12] The input bar is the data entry section located in the bottom-left corner of the screen. This is where your input appears as you type it.
[p44.b13] Mode, Network, and Time Status Bar
[p44.b14] This status bar in the top left of the screen is divided into three sections: mode, network, and time.
[p44.b15] 1 2 3
[p44.b16] The Mode, Network, and Time Status bar shows [1] the current machine mode, [2] network status icons, and [3] the current time.

=== page 45 ===
[p45.b1] 5.1 | MILL - CONTROL DISPLAY OVERVIEW
[p45.b2] Mode, Key Access, and Mode Display
[p45.b3] MODE [1]
[p45.b4] The Haas control organizes machine functions into three modes: Setup, Edit, and Operation. Each mode shows on one screen all of the information you need to do tasks under that mode. For example, in Setup mode, you have access to the work offset table, the tool offset table, and position information. Edit mode gives you access [p45.b5] to the program editor and optional systems like Visual Programming (VPS) (which contains Wireless Intuitive Probing (WIPS)). Operation mode includes Memory (MEM), the mode in which you run programs.
[p45.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: MODE | KEYS | DISPLAY [1] | FUNCTION
[p45.t1.r2] MODE: Setup | KEYS: ZERO RETURN | DISPLAY [1]: SETUP: ZERO | FUNCTION: Provides all control features for machine setup.
[p45.t1.r3] MODE (same as row above): Setup | KEYS: HANDLE JOG | DISPLAY [1]: SETUP: JOG | FUNCTION (same as row above): Provides all control features for machine setup.
[p45.t1.r4] MODE: Edit | KEYS: EDIT | DISPLAY [1]: ANY | FUNCTION: Provides all program editing, management, and transfer functions.
[p45.t1.r5] MODE (same as row above): Edit | KEYS: MDI | DISPLAY [1]: EDIT: MDI | FUNCTION (same as row above): Provides all program editing, management, and transfer functions.
[p45.t1.r6] MODE (same as row above): Edit | KEYS: LIST PROGRAM | DISPLAY [1]: ANY | FUNCTION (same as row above): Provides all program editing, management, and transfer functions.
[p45.t1.r7] MODE: Operation | KEYS: MEMORY | DISPLAY [1]: OPERATION: MEM | FUNCTION: Provides all control features necessary to run a program.
[p45.t1.r8] MODE (same as row above): Operation | KEYS: EDIT | DISPLAY [1]: OPERATION: MEM | FUNCTION: Provides background editing of active programs.
[p45.t1.r9] MODE (same as row above): Operation | KEYS: LIST PROGRAM | DISPLAY [1]: ANY | FUNCTION: Provides background editing of programs.

=== page 46 ===
[p46.b1] 5.1 | MILL - CONTROL DISPLAY OVERVIEW
[p46.b2] Network
[p46.b3] If you have networking installed on your Next Generation Control, icons in the center networking partition of the bar give you networking status. See the table for meanings of the networking icons.
[p46.t1.r1] column 1: The machine is connected to a wired network with an Ethernet cable.
[p46.t1.r2] column 1: The machine is connected to a wireless network with 70 - 100% signal strength.
[p46.t1.r3] column 1: The machine is connected to a wireless network with 30 - 70% signal strength.
[p46.t1.r4] column 1: The machine is connected to a wireless network with 1 - 30% signal strength.
[p46.t1.r5] column 1: The machine is connected to a wireless network, but it is not receiving data packets.
[p46.t1.r6] column 1: The machine is successfully registered with MyHaas and is communicating with the server.
[p46.t1.r7] column 1: The machine had previously registered with MyHaas and has a problem connecting to the server.
[p46.t1.r8] column 1: The machine is connected to a remote net share.
[p46.b4] Settings Display
[p46.b5] Press SETTING, then select the SETTINGS tab. Settings change the way the machine behaves; refer to the “Settings” section for a more detailed description.
[p46.b6] Coolant Display
[p46.b7] The coolant display appears in the upper-right of the screen in OPERATION:MEM mode.
[p46.b8] The first line tells you if the coolant is ON or OFF.
[p46.b9] The next line shows the position number of the optional Programmable Coolant Spigot (P-COOL). The positions are from 1 to 34. If the option is not installed, no position number appears.
[p46.b10] In the coolant gauge, a black arrow shows the coolant level. Full is 1/1 and empty is 0/1. To avoid coolant flow problems, keep the coolant level above the red range. You can also see this gauge in DIAGNOSTICS mode under the GAUGES tab.
[p46.b11] 46 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 47 ===
[p47.b1] 5.2 | MILL - POSITION DISPLAY
[p47.b2] Position Display
[p47.b3] The Position display shows the current axis position relative to four reference points (Work, Distance-to-go, Machine, and Operator). In any mode, press POSITION and use cursor [p47.b4] keys to access the different reference points displayed in tabs. The last tab display shows all the reference points on the same screen.
[p47.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: COORDINATE DISPLAY | FUNCTION
[p47.t1.r2] COORDINATE DISPLAY: WORK (G54) | FUNCTION: This tab displays the axis positions relative to part zero. On power-up, this position uses work offset G54 automatically. It displays the axis positions relative to the most recent­ ly-used work offset.
[p47.t1.r3] COORDINATE DISPLAY: DIST TO GO | FUNCTION: This tab displays the distance remaining before the axes reach their commanded po­ sition. When in SETUP:JOG mode, you can use this position display to show a distance moved. Change modes (MEM, MDI) and then switch back to SETUP:JOG mode to zero this value.
[p47.t1.r4] COORDINATE DISPLAY: MACHINE | FUNCTION: This tab displays the axis positions relative to machine zero.
[p47.t1.r5] COORDINATE DISPLAY: OPERATOR | FUNCTION: This tab shows the distance you have jogged the axes. This does not necessarily repre­ sent the actual distance the axis is from machine zero, except when the machine is first powered on.
[p47.t1.r6] COORDINATE DISPLAY: ALL | FUNCTION: This tab displays all reference points on the same screen.
[p47.b5] FUNCTION COORDINATE DISPLAY
[p47.b6] Axis Display Selection
[p47.b7] You can add or remove axes in the Positions displays. While a Positions display tab is active, press ALTER.
[p47.b8] The axis display selection window comes in from the right side of the screen.
[p47.b9] Use the cursor arrow keys to highlight an axis, and press ENTER to toggle it on and off for display. The positions display will show axes that have a check mark.
[p47.b10] Press ALTER to close the axis display selector.
[p47.b11] NOTE: You can display a maximum of (5) axes.

=== page 49 ===
[p49.b1] 5.4 | MILL - CURRENT COMMANDS
[p49.b2] Current Commands
[p49.b3] This section describes the Current Commands pages and the types of data they show. The information from most of these pages also appears in other modes.
[p49.b4] Press CURRENT COMMANDS to access the tabbed menu of available Current Commands displays.
[p49.b5] Devices - The Mechanisms tab on this page shows hardware devices on the machine that you can command manually. For example, you can manually extend and retract the Parts Catcher or Probe Arm. You can also manually rotate the spindle clockwise or counterclockwise at a desired RPM.
[p49.b6] Timers Display -This page shows:
[p49.b7] • The current date and time.
[p49.b8] • The total power on time.
[p49.b9] • Total cycle start time.
[p49.b10] • Total feed time.
[p49.b11] • M30 counters. Each time the a program reaches an M30 command, both of these counters increment by one.
[p49.b12] • Macro variable displays.
[p49.b13] You also see these timers and counters in the lower-right section of the display in the OPERATION:MEM, SETUP:ZERO, and EDIT:MDI modes.
[p49.b14] Macros Display - This page shows a list of the macro variables and their values. The control updates these variables as programs run. You can modify the variables in this display.
[p49.b15] Active Codes - This page lists the active program codes. A smaller version of this display is included on the OPERATION:MEM and EDIT:MDI mode screens. Also when you press PROGRAM in any Operation mode, you see the active program codes.
[p49.b16] Advanced Tool Management - This page contains information the control uses to predict tool life. Here is where you create and manage tool groups, and where you enter the maximum tool load percentage expected for each tool.
[p49.b17] For more information, refer to the Advanced Tool Management section in the Operation chapter of this manual.
[p49.b18] Calculator - This page contains the Standard, Milling/ Turning, and Tapping calculators.
[p49.b19] Media - This page contains the Media Player.
[p49.b20] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 49
=== page 50 ===
[p50.b1] 5.4 | MILL - CURRENT COMMANDS
[p50.b2] Devices - Mechanisms
[p50.b3] The Mechanisms page displays possible machine components and options on your machine. Select the listed mechanism using the UP and DOWN arrows for more information on its operation and use. Pages give detailed instructions on the functions of the machine
[p50.b4] Devices - Workholding
[p50.b5] Starting in software version 100.20.000.1110 a workholding tab was added to the control to support multiple workholding devices. The control will support Haas E-Vise [1], Hydraulic [2] and Pneumatic [3] vises.
[p50.b6] components, quick tips, as well as links to other pages to help you learn about and utilize your machine.
[p50.b7] • Select the Devices tab in the Current Commands menu.
[p50.b8] • Select the Mechanisms that you want to use.
[p50.b9] The Main Spindle option in Devices allows you to rotate the spindle clockwise or counterclockwise at a chosen RPM. The maximum RPM is limited by the machine’s maximum RPM settings.
[p50.b10] • Use the cursor arrow keys to move from field to field.
[p50.b11] • Enter the RPM you want to rotate the spindle and press F2.
[p50.b12] • Hold down F3 to rotate the spindle clockwise. Hold down F4 to rotate the spindle counterclockwise. The spindle comes to a stop when the button is released.
[p50.b13] The machine supports up to 3 foot pedals each would toggle Vise1, Vise2, and Vise3 respectively. If you have a single pedal you will need to enable Vise1 to the vise you want be actuated by the foot pedal.
[p50.b14] NOTE: The E-vise is used on the Mill APL and Robot systems but can also be used as a stand-alone product.
[p50.b15] You can actuate up to 8 workholding devices.
[p50.b16] To access the Workholding page press Current Commands and navigate to Devices > Workholding.
[p50.b17] From the Workholding display tab you will be able to:
[p50.b18] • Setup the workholding devices
[p50.b19] • Enable and Disable workholding devices
[p50.b20] • Clamp and Unclamp
[p50.b21] • Jog Advance / Retract (E-Vises only)
[p50.b22] 50 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 51 ===
[p51.b1] 5.4 | MILL - CURRENT COMMANDS
[p51.b2] Time Adjustment
[p51.b3] Follow this procedure to adjust the date or time.
[p51.b4] (step 1) 1. Select the Timers page in Current Commands.
[p51.b5] (step 2) 2. Use the cursor arrow keys to highlight the Date:, Time:, or Time Zone field.
[p51.b6] (step 3) 3. Press [EMERGENCY STOP].
[p51.b7] (step 4) 4. In the Date: field, type the new date in the format MM- DD-YYYY, including the hyphens.
[p51.b8] (step 5) 5. In the Time: field, type the new time in the format HH:MM, including the colon. Press [SHIFT] and then 9 to type the colon.
[p51.b9] Timer and Counter Reset
[p51.b10] You can reset the power-on, cycle-start, and feed cutting timers. You can also reset the M30 counters.
[p51.b11] (step 1) 1. Select the Timers page in Current Commands.
[p51.b12] (step 2) 2. Use the cursor arrow keys to highlight the name of the timer or counter that you want to reset.
[p51.b13] (step 3) 3. Press ORIGIN to reset the timer or counter.
[p51.b14] Current Commands - Active Codes
[p51.b15] (step 6) 6. In the Time Zone: field, press [ENTER] to select from the list of time zones. You can type search terms in the pop- up window to narrow the list. For example, type PST to find Pacific Standard Time. Highlight the time zone you want to use.
[p51.b16] (step 7) 7. Press [ENTER].
[p51.b17] tip: You can reset the M30 counters independently to track finished parts in two different ways; for example, parts finished in a shift and total parts finished.
[p51.b18] This display gives read-only, real-time information about the codes that are currently active in the program; specifically,
[p51.b19] • the codes that define the current motion type (rapid vs linear feed vs circular feed)
[p51.b20] • positioning system (absolute vs incremental)
[p51.b21] • cutter compensation (left, right or off)
[p51.b22] • active canned cycle, and work offset.
[p51.b23] This display also gives the active Dnn, Hnn, Tnn, and most recent M-code. If an alarm is active, this shows a quick display of the active alarm instead of the active codes.
[p51.b24] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 51
=== page 52 ===
[p52.b1] 5.4 | MILL - CURRENT COMMANDS
[p52.b2] Tools - Tool Table
[p52.b3] This section tells you how to use the tool table to give the control information about your tools.
[p52.b4] To access the Tool Pocket Table, press CURRENT COMMANDS and choose the Tool Table tab.
[p52.b5] Active Tool- Tells you the tool number that is installed in the spindle.
[p52.b6] Active Pocket - this show you the next pocket number.
[p52.b7] Set pocket as Large [L] - Use this flag when a large tool has a diameter of greater than 3” for 40-taper machines and greater than 4” for 50-taper machines. Scroll to the pocket of interest and press L to set the flag.
[p52.b8] CAUTION: You cannot place a large tool in the tool changer if one or both of the surrounding pockets already contain tools. Doing so causes the tool changer to crash. Large tools must have the surrounding pockets empty. However, large tools can share adjoining empty pockets.
[p52.b9] Set pocket as heavy [H] - Use this flag when a Heavy, Small Diameter 40-taper tool (4 lb or heavier) or a 50-taper tool (12 lb or heavier) is loaded into the spindle. Scroll to the pocket of interest and press H to set the flag.
[p52.b10] Set pocket as XL [X] - Use this flag when two adjacent pockets at each side of the tool are needed. Scroll to the pocket of interest and press X to set the flag.
[p52.b11] NOTE: This option only appears if your machine is a 50-taper.
[p52.b12] Clear category [Space] - Highlight the desired tool and press SPACE to clear the flag.
[p52.b13] Set tool [###] + [Enter]- Highlight the desired pocket and type in the tool number + Enter to set the desired tool number.
[p52.b14] NOTE: You cannot assign a tool number to more than one pocket. If you enter a tool number that is already defined in the tool pocket table, you see an Invalid tool error.
[p52.b15] Clear tool [0] + [Enter]- Highlight the desired pocket and press 0 + Enter to clear out the tool number.
[p52.b16] Reset table [Origin] - Press ORIGIN with the cursor in the center column to use the ORIGIN menu. This menu lets you:
[p52.b17] Sequence All Pockets - Makes all of the tool numbers sequential based on their pocket location, starting with 1.
[p52.b18] Zero All Pockets - Removes all of the tool numbers from all of the pocket numbers.
[p52.b19] Clear Category Flags - Removes the category designations from all of the tools.
[p52.b20] * Indicates current tool changer pocket.
[p52.b21] 52 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual

[p53.b1] 5.4 | MILL - CURRENT COMMANDS
[p53.b2] Tools - Tool Usage
[p53.b3] The Tool Usage tab contains information about the tools used in a program. This display will tell you information about each tool used in a program and statistics about each time it was used. It starts to collect information when user Main program start and clear information when meet codes M99, M299, M199.
[p53.b4] To get to the Tool Usage display press CURRENT COMMANDS then go to Tools and then Tool Usage tab.
[p53.b5] Start Time - When the tool was inserted to the spindle.
[p53.b6] Total Time - The total time the tool has been in the spindle.
[p53.b7] Feed time - Tool usage time.
[p53.b8] Load% - The maximum load of the Spindle during a tool usage.
[p53.b9] NOTE: This value is retrieved every second. The actual load compared to the recorded may vary.
[p53.b10] Feed/Total time - A graphic representation of the feed time of the tool over the total time.
[p53.b11] Engagement:
[p53.b12] • Black Bar- The tool usage versus another tools.
[p53.b13] • Gray Bar - This bar shows how long the tool was used in this usage related to other usages.
[p53.b14] Macros Interface You can use these macros variables to set and collect the tool usage data.

[p53.t1.r2] MACRO VARIABLE: #8608 | FUNCTION: Set the desired tool

[p53.t1.r10] MACRO VARIABLE: #8616 | FUNCTION: Usage Feed time
