=== page 54 ===
[p54.b1] 5.4 | MILL - CURRENT COMMANDS
[p54.b2] Tools - ATM
[p54.b3] Advanced Tool Management (ATM) lets you set up groups of duplicate tools for the same or a series of jobs.
[p54.b4] ATM classifies duplicate or backup tools into specific groups. In your program, you specify a group of tools instead of a single tool. ATM tracks the tool use in each tool group and compares it to your defined limits. When a tool reaches a limit, the control considers it “expired.” The next time your program calls that tool group, the control chooses a non-expired tool from the group.
[p54.b5] • When a tool expires:
[p54.b6] • The beacon will flash.
[p54.b7] • ATM puts the expired tool in the EXP group
[p54.b8] Tool groups that contain the tool appear with a red background.
[p54.b9] ALLOWED LIMITS This table gives data about all of the current tool groups, including default groups and user-specified groups. ALL is a default group that lists all of the tools in the system. EXP is a default group that lists all of the tools that are expired. The last row in the table shows all of the tools that are not assigned to tool groups. Use the cursor arrow keys or END to move the cursor to the row and see these tools.
[p54.b10] For each tool group in the ALLOWED LIMITS table, you define limits that determine when a tool expires. The limits apply to all tools assigned to this group. These limits affect every tool in the group.
[p54.b11] The columns in the ALLOWED LIMITS table are: GROUP - Displays the tool group’s ID number. This is the number you use to specify the tool group in a program.
[p54.b12] EXP # - Tells you how many tools in the group are expired. If you highlight the ALL row, you see a list of all of the expired tools in all groups.
[p54.b13] ORDER - Specifies the tool to use first. If you select ORDERED, ATM uses the tools in tool number order. You can also have ATM automatically use the NEWEST or OLDEST tool in the group.
[p54.b14] USAGE - The maximum number of times the control can use a tool before it expires.
[p54.b15] HOLES - The maximum number of holes a tool is allowed to drill before it expires.
[p54.b16] WARN - The minimum value for tool life remaining in the group before the control gives a warning message.
[p54.b17] LOAD - The allowed load limit for tools in the group before the control does the ACTION that the next column specifies.
[p54.b18] ACTION - The automatic action when a tool reaches its maximum tool load percentage. Highlight the tool action box to change and press ENTER. Use the UP and DOWN cursor keys to select the automatic action from the pull down menu (ALARM, FEEDHOLD, BEEP, AUTOFEED, NEXT TOOL).
[p54.b19] To use ATM, press CURRENT COMMANDS, and then select ATM in the tabbed menu. The ATM window has two sections: Allowed Limits and Tool Data.
[p54.b20] FEED - The total amount of time, in minutes, that the tool can be in a feed.
[p54.b21] TOTAL TIME - The total amount of time, in minutes, that the control can use a tool. TOOL DATA This table gives information about each tool in a tool group. To look at a group, highlight it in the ALLOWED LIMITS table, and then press F4.
[p54.b22] TOOL# - Shows the tool numbers used in the group.
[p54.b23] LIFE - The percentage of life left in a tool. This is calculated by the CNC control, using actual tool data and the allowed limits the operator entered for the group.
[p54.b24] USAGE - The total number of times that a program has called the tool (number of tool changes).
[p54.b25] HOLES - The number of holes the tool has drilled/ tapped/ bored.
[p54.b26] LOAD - The maximum load, in percent, exerted on the tool.
[p54.b27] LIMIT - The maximum load allowed for the tool
[p54.b28] FEED - The amount of time, in minutes, the tool has been in a feed.
[p54.b29] TOTAL - The total amount of time, in minutes, the tool has been used.
[p54.b30] H-CODE - The tool-length code to use for the tool. You can edit this only if Setting 15 is set to OFF.
[p54.b31] D-CODE - The diameter code to use for the tool.
[p54.b32] NOTE: By default, the H- and D-codes in Advanced Tool Management are set to equal the tool number that is added to the group.
[p54.b33] 54 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 55 ===
[p55.b1] 5.4 | MILL - CURRENT COMMANDS
[p55.b2] Plane
[p55.b3] The Plane tab allows for a machine with a Gimbal Spindle [p55.b4] define custom planes for jogging.
[p55.b5] The plane tab can used in conjunction with G268 run in a [p55.b6] program or from filling out the required fields.
[p55.b7] Each one of the required fields has a help text at the bottom of [p55.b8] the table.
[p55.b9] To enter plane jogging mode type in “PJ” followed by
[p55.b10] [HAND JOG].
[p55.b11] Calculator
[p55.b12] The calculator tab includes calculators for basic mathematical functions, milling, and tapping.
[p55.b13] • Select the calculator tab in the Current Commands menu.
[p55.b14] • Select the calculator tab that you want to use: Standard, Milling, or Tapping.
[p55.b15] The standard calculator has functions like a simple desktop calculator; with available operations such as addition, subtraction, multiplication, and division, as well as square root and percentage. The calculator lets you easily transfer operations and results to the input line so that you can put them into programs. You can also transfer results into the Milling and Tapping calculators.
[p55.b16] Use the number keys to type operands into the calculator.
[p55.b17] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 55
=== page 56 ===
[p56.b1] 5.4 | MILL - CURRENT COMMANDS
[p56.b2] Calculator (Contin.)
[p56.b3] To insert an arithmetic operator, use the letter key that appears in brackets next to the operator you want to insert. These keys are:
[p56.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: KEY | FUNCTION | KEY | FUNCTION
[p56.t1.r2] KEY: D | FUNCTION: Add | KEY: K | FUNCTION: Square Root
[p56.t1.r3] KEY: J | FUNCTION: Subtract | KEY: Q | FUNCTION: Percentage
[p56.t1.r4] KEY: P | FUNCTION: Multiply | KEY: S | FUNCTION: Memory Store (MS)
[p56.t1.r5] KEY: V | FUNCTION: Divide | KEY: R | FUNCTION: Memory Recall (MR)
[p56.t1.r6] KEY: E | FUNCTION: Toggle sign (+/-) | KEY: C | FUNCTION: Memory Clear (MC)
[p56.b4] FUNCTION KEY KEY FUNCTION
[p56.b5] After you have entered data into the calculator input field, you can do any of the following:
[p56.b6] NOTE: These options are available for all calculators.
[p56.b7] • Press ENTER to return the result of your calculation.
[p56.b8] • Press INSERT to append the data or the result to the end of the input line.
[p56.b9] • Press ALTER to move the data or the result to the input line. This overwrites the current contents of the input line.
[p56.b10] • Press ORIGIN to reset the calculator.
[p56.b11] Keep the data or the result in the calculator input field and select a different calculator tab. The data in the calculator input field remains available to transfer into the other calculators.
[p56.b12] 56 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 57 ===
[p57.b1] 5.4 | MILL - CURRENT COMMANDS
[p57.b2] Milling /Turning Calculator
[p57.b3] The milling/turning calculator lets you automatically calculate machining parameters based on given information. When you have entered enough information, the calculator automatically displays results in the relevant fields. These fields are marked with an asterisk ( * ).
[p57.b4] • Use the cursor arrow keys to move from field to field.
[p57.b5] • Type known values in the appropriate fields. You can also press F3 to copy a value from the standard calculator.
[p57.b6] • In the Work Material and Tool Material fields, use the LEFT and RIGHT cursor arrow keys to choose from the available options.
[p57.b7] • wCalculated values appear highlighted in yellow when they are outside of the recommended range for the workpiece and tool material. Also, when all of the calculator fields contain data (calculated or entered), the milling calculator displays the recommended power for the operation.
[p57.b8] Tapping Calculator
[p57.b9] The tapping calculator lets you automatically calculate tapping parameters based on given information. When you have entered enough information, the calculator automatically displays results in the relevant fields. These fields are marked with an asterisk ( * ).
[p57.b10] • Use the cursor arrow keys to move from field to field.
[p57.b11] • Type known values in the appropriate fields. You can also press F3 to copy a value from the standard calculator.
[p57.b12] • When the calculator has enough information, it puts calculated values in the appropriate fields.
[p57.b13] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 57
=== page 58 ===
[p58.b1] 5.4 | MILL - CURRENT COMMANDS
[p58.b2] Media Display
[p58.b3] M130 Lets you display video with audio and still images during program execution. Some examples of how you can use this feature are:
[p58.b4] NOTE: M130 uses the subprogram search settings, Settings 251 and 252 in the same way that M98 does. You can also use the Insert Media File command in the editor to easily insert an M130 code that includes the file path. Refer to page 5 for more information.
[p58.b5] Providing visual cues or work instructions during program operation
[p58.b6] $FILE Lets you display video with audio and still images outside of program execution.
[p58.b7] Providing images to aid part inspection at certain points in a program
[p58.b8] The correct command format is ( $FILE file.xxx), where file. xxx is the name of the file, plus the path, if necessary. You can also add a comment between the first parentheses and the dollar sign to appear as a comment in the media window.
[p58.b9] Demonstrating procedures with video
[p58.b10] The correct command format is M130(file.xxx), where file. xxx is the name of the file, plus the path, if necessary. You can also add a second comment in parentheses to appear as a comment in the media window.
[p58.b11] To display the media file, highlight the block while in memory mode and press enter. $FILE media display block will be ignored as comments during program execution.
[p58.b12] Example: M130(Remove Lifing Bolts Before Starting Op 2) (User Data/My Media/loadOp2.png);
[p58.b13] Example: (Remove Lifing Bolts Before Starting Op 2 $FILE User Data/My Media/loadOp2.png);
[p58.t1.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: STANDARD | PROFILE | RESOLUTION | BITRATE
[p58.t1.r2] STANDARD: MPEG-2 | PROFILE: Main-High | RESOLUTION: 1080 i/p, 30 fps | BITRATE: 50 Mbps
[p58.t1.r3] STANDARD: MPEG-4 / XviD | PROFILE: SP/ASP | RESOLUTION: 1080 i/p, 30 fps | BITRATE: 40 Mbps
[p58.t1.r4] STANDARD: H.263 | PROFILE: P0/P3 | RESOLUTION: 16 CIF, 30fps | BITRATE: 50 Mbps
[p58.t1.r5] STANDARD: DivX | PROFILE: 3/4/5/6 | RESOLUTION: 1080 i/p, 30fps | BITRATE: 40 Mbps
[p58.t1.r6] STANDARD: Baseline | PROFILE: 8192 x 8192 | RESOLUTION: 120 Mpixel/sec | BITRATE: -
[p58.t1.r7] STANDARD: PNG | PROFILE: - | RESOLUTION: - | BITRATE: -
[p58.t1.r8] STANDARD: JPEG | PROFILE: - | RESOLUTION: - | BITRATE: -
[p58.b14] BITRATE STANDARD RESOLUTION PROFILE
[p58.b15] NOTE: For the fastest loading times, use files with pixel dimensions divisible by 8 (most unedited digital images have these dimensions by default), and a maximum resolution of 1920 x 1080.
[p58.b16] Example: (Remove Lifing Bolts Before Starting Op 2 $FILE User Data/My Media/loadOp2.png);
[p58.b17] Your media appears in the Media tab under Current Commands. The media displays until the next M130 displays a different file, or M131 clears the media tab contents.
[p58.b18] 58 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 59 ===
[p59.b1] 5.5 | MILL - ALARMS & MESSAGES
[p59.b2] Alarms and Messages Display
[p59.b3] Use this display to learn more about machine alarms when they occur, view your machine’s entire alarm history, look up definitions of alarms that can occur, view created messages, and show the keystroke history.
[p59.b4] Press ALARMS, then select a display tab:
[p59.b5] The ACTIVE ALARM tab shows the alarms that currently affect machine operation. Use PAGE UP and PAGE DOWN to see the other active alarms.
[p59.b6] The MESSAGES tab shows the messages page. The text you put on this page stays there when you power the machine off. You can use this to leave messages and information for the next machine operator, etc.
[p59.b7] Add Messages
[p59.b8] You can save a message in the MESSAGES tab. Your message stays there until you remove it or change it, even when you turn the machine off.
[p59.b9] (step 1) 1. Press ALARMS, select the MESSAGES tab, and press the DOWN cursor arrow key.
[p59.b10] (step 2) 2. Type your message. Press CANCEL to backspace and delete. Press DELETE to delete an entire line. Press ERASE PROGRAM to delete the entire message.
[p59.b11] The ALARM HISTORY tab shows a list of the alarms that have recently affected machine operation. You can also search for an alarm number or alarm text. To do this type in the alarm number or the desired text and press F1.
[p59.b12] The ALARM VIEWER tab shows a detailed description of all the alarms. You can also search for an alarm number or alarm text. To do this type in the alarm number or the desired text and press F1.
[p59.b13] The KEY HISTORY tab shows up to the last 2000 keystrokes.
[p59.b14] Example: (Remove Lifing Bolts Before Starting Op 2 $FILE User Data/My Media/loadOp2.png);
[p59.b15] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 59

=== page 61 ===
[p61.b1] 6.1 | MILL - DEVICE MANAGER OVERVIEW
[p61.b2] Device Manager (List Program)
[p61.b3] You use the device manager (LIST PROGRAM) to access, save, and manage data on the CNC control, and on other devices attached to the control. You also use the device manager to load and transfer programs between devices, set your active program, and back up your machine data.
[p61.b4] In the tabbed menu at the top of the display, the device manager (LIST PROGRAM) shows you only the available memory devices. For example, if you do not have a USB memory device connected to the control pendant, the tabbed menu does not show a USB tab. For more information on navigating tabbed menus, refer to Chapter 5.1.
[p61.b5] The device manager (LIST PROGRAM) shows you the available data in a directory structure. At the root of the CNC control are the available memory devices in a tabbed menu. Each device can contain combinations of directories and files, many levels deep. This is similar to the file structure you find in common personal computer operating systems.
[p61.b6] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 61
=== page 62 ===
[p62.b1] 6.2 | MILL - DEVICE MANAGER OVERVIEW
[p62.b2] Device Manager Operation
[p62.b3] Press LIST PROGRAM to access the device manager. The initial device manager display shows the available memory devices in a tabbed menu. These devices can include machine memory, the User Data directory, USB memory devices connected to the control, and files available on the connected network. Select a device tab to work with the files on that device.
[p62.b4] Device Manager Initial Screen Example:
[p62.b5] [1] Available Device Tabs,
[p62.b6] [2] Search Box,
[p62.b7] [3] Function Keys,
[p62.b8] [4] File Display,
[p62.b9] [5] File Comments (only available in Memory).
[p62.b10] Use the cursor arrow keys to navigate the directory structure:
[p62.b11] • Use the UP and DOWN cursor arrow keys to highlight and interact with a file or a directory in the current root or directory.
[p62.b12] • Roots and directories have a right-hand arrow character (>) in the far-right column of the file display. Use the RIGHT cursor arrow key to open a highlighted root or directory. The display then shows the contents of that root or directory.
[p62.b13] • Use the LEFT cursor arrow key to return to the previous root or directory. The display then shows the contents of that root or directory
[p62.b14] • The CURRENT DIRECTORY message above the file display tells you where you are in the directory structure; for example: MEMORY/CUSTOMER 11/NEW PROGRAMS shows that you are in the subdirectory NEW_PROGRAMS inside the directory CUSTOMER 11, in the root of MEMORY.

=== page 63 ===
[p63.b1] 6.3 | MILL - FILE DISPLAY
[p63.b2] File Display Columns
[p63.b3] When you open a root or directory with the RIGHT cursor arrow key, the file display shows you a list of the files and directories in that directory. Each column in the file display has information about the files or directories in the list.
[p63.b4] The columns are:
[p63.b5] • File selection check box (no label): Press ENTER to toggle a check mark on and off in the box. A check mark in a box indicates that the file or directory selected for operations on multiple files (usually copy or delete).
[p63.b6] • Program O Number (O #): This column lists the program numbers of the programs in the directory. The letter ‘O’ is omitted in the column data. Only available in the Memory tab.
[p63.b7] • File comment (Comment): This column lists the optional program comment that appears in the first line of the program. Only available in the Memory tab.
[p63.b8] • File Name (File Name): This is an optional name that the control uses when you copy the file to a memory device other than the control. For example, if you copy program O00045 to a USB memory device, the filename in the USB directory is NEXTGENtest.nc.
[p63.b9] • Size (Size): This column shows the amount of storage space that the file takes up. Directories in the list have the designation <DIR> in this column.
[p63.b10] NOTE: This column is hidden by default, press the F3 button and select Show File Details to display this column.
[p63.b11] • Date Last Modified (Last Modified): This column shows the last date and time when the file was changed. The format is YYYY/MM/DD HR:MIN.
[p63.b12] NOTE: This column is hidden by default, press the F3 button and select Show File Details to display this column.
[p63.b13] • Other Information (no label): This column gives you some information about a file’s status. The active program has an asterisk (*) in this column. A letter E in this column means that the program is in the program editor. A greater-than symbol (>) indicates a directory. A letter S indicates that a directory is part of Setting 252. Use the RIGHT or LEFT cursor arrow keys to enter or exit the directory.
[p63.b14] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 63
=== page 64 ===
[p64.b1] 6.3 | MILL - FILE DISPLAY
[p64.b2] Check Mark Selection
[p64.b3] The check box column on the far left of the file display lets you select multiple files.
[p64.b4] Press ENTER to place a check mark in a file’s check box. Highlight another file and press ENTER again to put a check mark in that file’s check box. Repeat this process until you have selected all of the files you want to select.
[p64.b5] You can then do an operation (usually copy or delete) on all of those files at the same time. Each file that is part of your selection has a check mark in the check box. When you choose an operation, the control does that operation on all of the files with check marks.
[p64.b6] For example, if you want to copy a set of files from the machine memory to a USB memory device, you would put a check mark on all of the files that you want to copy, then press F2 to start the copy operation.
[p64.b7] To delete a set of files, put a check mark on all of files you want to delete, then press DELETE to start the delete operation.
[p64.b8] Select the Active Program
[p64.b9] Highlight a program in the memory directory, then press SELECT PROGRAM to make the highlighted program active.
[p64.b10] The active program has an asterisk (*) in the far-right column in the file display. It is the program that runs when you press CYCLE START in OPERATION:MEM mode. The program is also protected from deletion while it is active.
[p64.b11] NOTE: A check-mark selection only marks the file for further operation; it does not make the program active.
[p64.b12] NOTE: If you have not selected multiple files with check marks, the control does operations only on the currently highlighted directory or file. If you have selected files, the control does operations only on the selected files and not on the highlighted file, unless it is also selected.
[p64.b13] 64 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 65 ===
[p65.b1] 6.4 | MILL - CREATE, EDIT, COPY A PROGRAM
[p65.b2] Create a New Program
[p65.b3] Press INSERT to create a new file in the current directory. The CREATE NEW PROGRAM popup menu shows on the screen:
[p65.b4] Create New Program Popup Menu Example: [1] Program O number field, [2] File Name field, [3] File comment field.
[p65.t1.r2] column 1: 1
[p65.t1.r4] column 1: 2
[p65.t1.r6] column 1: 3
[p65.b5] Enter the new program information in the fields. The Program O number field is required; the File Name and File comment are optional. Use the UP and DOWN cursors to move between the menu fields.
[p65.b6] Press UNDO at any time to cancel program creation.
[p65.b7] • Program O number (required for files created in Memory): Enter a program number up to (5) digits long. The control adds the letter O automatically. If you enter a number shorter than (5) digits, the control adds leading zeros to the program number to make it (5) digits long; for example, if you enter 1, the control adds zeros to make it 00001.
[p65.b8] NOTE: Do not use O09XXX numbers when you create new programs. Macro programs often use numbers in this block, and overwriting them may cause machine functions to malfunction or stop working.
[p65.b9] File comment (optional): Type a descriptive program title. This title goes into the program as a comment in the first line with the O number.
[p65.b10] Press ENTER to save your new program. If you specified an O number that exists in the current directory, the control gives the message File with O Number nnnnn already exists. Do you want to replace it? Press ENTER to save the program and overwrite the existing program, press CANCEL to return to the program name popup, or press UNDO to cancel.
[p65.b11] File Name (optional): Type a filename for the new program. This is the name the control uses when you copy the program to a storage device other than memory.
[p65.b12] Edit a Program
[p65.b13] Highlight a program, and then press ALTER to move the program into the program editor.
[p65.b14] The program has the designation E in the far-right column of the file display list when it is in the editor, unless it is also the active program.
[p65.b15] You can use this function to edit a program while the active program runs. You can edit the active program, but your changes do not take effect until you save the program and then select it again in the device manager menu.
[p65.b16] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 65
=== page 66 ===
[p66.b1] 6.4 | MILL - CREATE, EDIT, COPY A PROGRAM
[p66.b2] Copy Programs
[p66.b3] This function lets you copy programs to a device or a different directory.
[p66.b4] To copy a single program, highlight it in the device manager program list and press ENTER to assign a check mark. To copy multiple programs, check-mark all the programs you want to copy.
[p66.b5] Press F2 to start the copy operation.
[p66.b6] The Select Device popup appears.
[p66.b7] Select Device
[p66.b8] Use the cursor arrow keys to select the destination directory. RIGHT cursor to enter the chosen directory.
[p66.b9] Press ENTER to complete the copy operation, or press CANCEL to return to the device manager.
[p66.b10] 66 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 67 ===
[p67.b1] 6.5 | MILL - PROGRAM EDITING
[p67.b2] Create / Select Programs for Editing
[p67.b3] You use the Device Manager (LIST PROGRAM) to create and select programs for editing. Refer to the CREATE, EDIT, COPY A PROGRAM tab to create a new program.
[p67.b4] Program Edit Modes
[p67.b5] You use the Device Manager (LIST PROGRAM) to create and select programs for editing. Refer to the CREATE, EDIT, COPY A PROGRAM tab to create a new program.
[p67.b6] The Haas control has (2) program edit modes: The program editor or manual data input (MDI). You use the program editor to make changes to numbered programs stored in an attached memory device (machine memory, USB, or net share). You use MDI mode to command the machine without a formal program.
[p67.b7] The Haas control screen has (2) program edit panes: The Active Program / MDI pane, and the Program Generation pane. The Active Program / MDI pane is on the left side of the screen in all display modes. The Program Generation pane appears only in EDIT mode.

[p67.b9] [1] Active Program / MDI Pane,
[p67.b10] [2] Program Edit Pane,

=== page 68 ===
[p68.b1] 6.5 | MILL - PROGRAM EDITING
[p68.b2] Basic Program Editing
[p68.b3] This section describes the basic program editing functions. These functions are available when you edit a program.
[p68.b4] (step 1) 1) To write a program, or make changes to a program:
[p68.b5] • To edit a program in MDI, press MDI. This is EDIT:MDI mode. The program is displayed on the Active pane.
[p68.b6] • To edit a numbered program, select it in the Device Manager (LIST PROGRAM), then press EDIT. This is EDIT:EDIT mode. The program is displayed in the Program Generation pane.
[p68.b7] (step 2) 2) To highlight code:
[p68.b8] • Use the cursor arrow keys or the jog handle to move the highlight cursor through the program.
[p68.b9] • You can interact with single pieces of code or text (cursor highlighting), blocks of code, or multiple blocks of code (block selection). Refer to the Block Selection section for more information.
[p68.b10] (step 3) 3) To add code to the program:
[p68.b11] • Highlight the code block you want the new code to follow.
[p68.b12] • Type the new code.
[p68.b13] • Press INSERT. Your new code appears after the block you highlighted.
[p68.b14] (step 4) 4) To replace code:
[p68.b15] • Highlight the code you want to replace.
[p68.b16] • Type the code you want to replace the highlighted code with.
[p68.b17] • Press ALTER. Your new code takes the place of the code you highlighted.
[p68.b18] (step 5) 5) To remove characters or commands:
[p68.b19] • Highlight the text you want to delete.
[p68.b20] • Press DELETE. The text that you highlighted is removed from the program.
[p68.b21] (step 6) 6) Press UNDO to reverse up to the last (40) changes.
[p68.b22] NOTE: You cannot use UNDO to reverse changes that you made if you exit EDIT:EDIT mode.
[p68.b23] NOTE: In EDIT:EDIT mode, the control does not save the program as you edit. Press MEMORY to save the program and load it into the Active Program pane.
[p68.b24] 68 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 69 ===
[p69.b1] 6.5 | MILL - PROGRAM EDITING
[p69.b2] Block Selection
[p69.b3] When you edit a program, you can select single or multiple blocks of code. You can then copy and paste, delete, or move those blocks in one step.
[p69.b4] To select a block:
[p69.b5] • Use the cursor arrow keys to move the highlight cursor to the first or last block in your selection.
[p69.b6] NOTE: You can start a selection at the top block or the bottom block, and then move up or down as appropriate to complete your selection.
[p69.b7] Actions with a Block Selection
[p69.b8] After you make a text selection, you can copy and paste it, move it, or delete it.
[p69.b9] NOTE: These instructions assume you have already made a block selection as described in the Block Selection section.
[p69.b10] NOTE: These are actions available in MDI and the Program Editor. You cannot use UNDO to reverse these actions.
[p69.b11] (step 1) 1) To copy and paste the selection:
[p69.b12] • Move the cursor to the location where you want to put a copy of the text.
[p69.b13] • Press ENTER.
[p69.b14] The control puts a copy of the selection on the next line after the cursor location.
[p69.b15] NOTE: The control does not copy the text to the clipboard when you use this function.
[p69.b16] NOTE: You cannot include the program name block in your selection. The control gives the message GUARDED CODE.
[p69.b17] • Press F2 to start your selection.
[p69.b18] • Use the cursor arrow keys or the jog handle to expand the selection.
[p69.b19] • Press F2 to complete the selection.
[p69.b20] (step 2) 2) To move the selection:
[p69.b21] • Move the cursor to the location where you want to move the text.
[p69.b22] • Press ALTER.
[p69.b23] The control removes the text from its current location and puts it in the line after the current line.
[p69.b24] (step 3) 3) Press DELETE to delete the selection.
[p69.b25] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 69
=== page 70 ===
[p70.b1] 7.1 | MILL - TOUCHSCREEN OVERVIEW
[p70.b2] LCD Touchscreen Overview
[p70.b3] The touchscreen feature allows you to navigate the control in a more intuitive way.
[p70.b4] NOTE: If the touchscreen hardware is not detected at power on, a notification 20016 Touchscreen not detected will appear in the alarm history.
[p70.b5] Touchscreen Status Icons
[p70.b6] SETTINGS
[p70.b7] 381 - Enable / Disable Touchscreen
[p70.b8] 383- Table Row Size
[p70.b9] 396 - Virtual Keyboard Enabled

=== page 77 ===
[p77.b1] 8.2 | MILL - JOG MODE
[p77.b2] Jog Mode
[p77.b3] Jog mode lets you jog the machine axes to a desired location. Before you can jog an axis, the machine must establish its home position. The control does this at machine power-up.
[p77.b4] To enter jog mode:
[p77.b5] (step 1) 1. Press HANDLE JOG.
[p77.b6] (step 2) 2. Press the desired axis (+X, -X, +Y,-Y, +Z, -Z, +A/C or -A/C, +B, or -B).
[p77.b7] (step 3) 3. There are different increment speeds that can be used while in jog mode; they are .0001, .001, .01 and .1. Each click of the jog handle moves the axis the distance defined by the current jog rate. You can also use an optional Remote Jog Handle (RJH) to jog the axes.
[p77.b8] (step 4) 4. Press and hold the handle jog buttons or use the jog handle control to move the axis.
[p77.b9] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 77
=== page 78 ===
[p78.b1] 8.3 | MILL - TOOL OFFSETS
[p78.b2] Tool Offsets
[p78.b3] To machine a part accurately, the mill needs to know where the part is located on the table and the distance from the tip of the tools to the top of the part (tool offset from home position).
[p78.b4] The tool offset behavior has been modified on Haas machines in the following ways:
[p78.b5] • By default tool offsets will now always be applied, unless a G49/H00 (Mill) or Txx00 offset (Lathe) is explicitly specified.
[p78.b6] • On mills, when a tool change occurs, the tool offset will automatically update to match the new tool. This behavior already existed on lathes.
[p78.b7] (step 1) 1) Active Tool: - This tells you which tool is in the spindle.
[p78.b8] (step 2) 2) Tool Offset (T) - This is the list of tool offsets. There is a maximum of 200 tool offsets available.
[p78.b9] (step 3) 3) Length Geometry (H), Length Wear (H) - These two columns are tied to the G43 (H) values in the program. If you command a G43 H01; from within a program for tool #1, the program will use the values from these columns.
[p78.b10] NOTE: The Length Geometry can be set manually or automatically by the probe.
[p78.b11] Press the OFFSET button to view the tool offset values. The tool offsets can be entered manually or automatically with a probe. The list below will shows how each offset setting works.
[p78.b12] (step 4) 4) Diameter Geometry (D), Diameter Wear (D) - These two columns are used for cutter compensation. If you command a G41 D01;
[p78.b13] from within a program, the program will use the values from these columns.
[p78.b14] NOTE: The Diameter Geometry can be set manually or automatically by the probe.
[p78.b15] (step 5) 5) Coolant Position - Use this column for setting the coolant position for the tool in this row.
[p78.b16] NOTE: This column will only show if you have the Programmable Coolant option.
[p78.b17] (step 6) 6) These functions buttons allow you to set the offset values.
[p78.b18] 78 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 79 ===
[p79.b1] 8.3 | MILL - TOOL OFFSETS
[p79.b2] (step 7) 7) Flutes - When this column is set to the correct value, the control can calculate the correct Chip Load value displayed at the Main Spindle screen. The VPS feeds and speeds library will also use these values for calculations.
[p79.b3] NOTE: The values set on the Flute column will not affect the operation of the probe.
[p79.b4] (step 8) 8) Actual Diameter - This column is used by the control to calculate the correct Surface Speed value displayed at the Main Spindle screen.
[p79.b5] (step 9) 9) Tool Type - This column is used by the control to decide which probe cycle to use to probe this tool. Press F1 to view the options: None, Drill, Tap, Shell Mill, End Mill, Spot Drill, Ball Nose and Probe. When this field is set to Drill, Tap, Spot Drill, Ball Nose and Probe, the probe will probe along the tool centerline for the length. When this field is set to Shell Mill or End Mill the probe will probe at the tools edge.
[p79.b6] (step 10) 10) Tool Material - This column is used for calculations by the VPS feeds and speeds library. Press F1 to view the options: User, Carbide, Steel. Press Enter to set the material, or press Cancel to exit.
[p79.b7] (step 11) 11) Tool Pocket - This column shows you what pocket the tool is currently in. This column is read only.
[p79.b8] (step 12) 12) Tool Category - This column shows if the tool is set up as large, heavy or extra large. To make a change, highlight the column and press ENTER. The Tool Table will be dis­ played. Follow the instructions on the screen to make tool table changes.
[p79.b9] (step 13) 13) Approximate Length - This column is used by the probe. The value in this field tells the probe the distance from the tip of the tool to the spindle gauge line.
[p79.b10] NOTE: If you probe the length of a drill or a tap, or some tool that is not a shell mill or an end mill you can leave this field blank.
[p79.b11] (step 14) 14) Approximate Diameter - This column is used by the probe. The value in this field tells the probe the diameter of the tool.
[p79.b12] (step 15) 15) Edge Measure Height - This column is used by the probe. The value in this field is the distance below the tip of the tool that the tool needs to move, when the tool diameter is probed. Use this setting when you have a tool with a large radius or when you are probing a diameter on a chamfer tool.
[p79.b13] (step 16) 16) Tool Tolerance - This column is used by the probe. The value in this field is used for checking tool breakage and wear detection. Leave this field blank if you are setting the length and diameter on the tool.
[p79.b14] (step 17) 17) Probe Type - This column is used by the probe. You can select the probe routine you want to perform on this tool.
[p79.b15] The choices are: 0 - No tool probing to be performed., 1- Length probing (Rotating)., 2 - Length probing (Non-Ro­ tating)., 3 - Length and Diameter probing (Rotating). Press TOOL OFFSET MEASURE to set automatic probe options.
[p79.b16] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 79
=== page 80 ===
[p80.b1] 8.4 | MILL - SET A TOOL OFFSET
[p80.b2] Set a Tool Offset
[p80.b3] The next step is to touch off the tools. This defines the distance from the tip of the tool to the top of the part. Another name for this is Tool Length Offset, which is designated as H in a line of machine code. The distance for each tool is entered into the TOOL OFFSET table.
[p80.b4] NOTE: When touching off tools or working on the stationary table, make sure the tilt axis is at 0 degrees (A0° or B0°).
[p80.b5] 1
[p80.b6] 2
[p80.b7] • Place a sheet of paper between the tool and the work piece. Carefully move the tool down to the top of the part, as close as possible, and still be able to move the paper.
[p80.b8] Setting Tool Offset. With the Z Axis at its home position, Tool Length Offset is measured from the tip of the tool [1] to the top of the part [2].
[p80.b9] • Load the tool in the spindle [1].
[p80.b10] • Press OFFSET [D] and select the TOOL tab.
[p80.b11] • Press HANDLE JOG [F].
[p80.b12] • Highlight the H (length) Geometry value for position #1.
[p80.b13] • Press .1/100. [G] (The mill moves at a fast rate when the handle is turned).
[p80.b14] • Press TOOL OFFSET MEASURE [A]. A pop box may appear if the change is Greater Than Setting 142! Accept (Y/N). Press Y to accept.
[p80.b15] • Select between the X and Y axes [J], and with the use the jog handle [K] to move the tool near the center of the part.
[p80.b16] • CAUTION: The next step causes the spindle to move rapidly in the Z Axis.
[p80.b17] • Press +Z [C].
[p80.b18] • Press NEXT TOOL [B].
[p80.b19] • Jog the Z Axis approximately 1” above the part.
[p80.b20] • Repeat the offset process for each tool.
[p80.b21] • Press .0001/.1 [H] (The mill moves at a slow rate when the handle is turned).
[p80.b22] 80 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual

[p81.b1] 8.5 | MILL - WORK OFFSETS
[p81.b2] Work Offsets
[p81.b3] Press the OFFSET, then the F4 to view the work offset values. The work offsets can be entered manually or automatically with a probe. The list below will shows how [p81.b4] each work offset setting works.
[p81.b5] (step 1) 1) G Code - This column displays all the available work offset G-codes. For more information on these work offsets, refer to the G52 Set Work Coordinate System (Group 00 or 12), G54 Work Offsets, G92 Set Work Coordinate Systems Shift Value (Group 00).
[p81.b6] (step 2) 2) X, Y, Z, Axis - This column displays the work offset value for each axis. If rotary axis are enabled the offsets for these will be displayed on this page.
[p81.b7] (step 3) 3) Work Material - This column is used by the VPS feeds and speeds library.
[p81.b8] (step 4) 4) These functions buttons allow you to set the offset values. Type in the desired work offset value and press F1 to set the value. Press F3 to set a probing action. Press F4 to toggle from work to tool offset tab. Type in a value and press Enter to add to the current value.

=== page 82 ===
[p82.b1] 8.6 | MILL - SET A WORK OFFSET
[p82.b2] Set a Work Offset
[p82.b3] To machine a workpiece, the mill needs to know where the workpiece is located on the table. You can use an edge finder, an electronic probe, or many other tools and methods to establish part zero. To set the part zero offset with a mechanical pointer:
[p82.b4] 1
[p82.b5] 2
[p82.b6] Place the material [1] in the vise and tighten.
[p82.b7] Navigate to the OFFSET>WORK [C] tab and press the DOWN cursor key [H] to activate the page. You can press F4 to toggle between Tool offsets and Work offsets.
[p82.b8] Load a pointer tool [2] in the spindle.
[p82.b9] Press HANDLE JOG [E].
[p82.b10] Navigate to the G54 X Axis location.
[p82.b11] Press .1/100. [F] (The mill moves at a fast speed when the handle is turned).
[p82.b12] CAUTION: In the next step, do not press PART ZERO SET a third time; this loads a value into the Z AXIS column. This causes a crash or a Z-Axis alarm when the program is run.
[p82.b13] Press +Z [A].
[p82.b14] Press PART ZERO SET [B] to load the value into the X Axis column. The second press of PART ZERO SET [B] loads the value into the Y Axis column.
[p82.b15] Use the jog handle [J] to move the Z-Axis approximately 1” above the part.
[p82.b16] Press .001/1. [G] (The mill moves at a slow speed when the handle is turned).
[p82.b17] Jog the Z-Axis approximately. 0.2” above the part.
[p82.b18] Select between the X and Y axes [I] and jog the tool to the upper left corner of the part (see illustration [9])
[p82.b19] 82 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual

=== page 84 ===
[p84.b1] 9.1 | MILL - UMBRELLA TOOL CHANGER OVERVIEW
[p84.b2] Tool Changers
[p84.b3] There are (2) types of mill tool changers: the umbrella style (UTC), and the side-mount tool changer (SMTC). You command both tool changers in the same way, but you set them up differently.
[p84.b4] Use TOOL RELEASE, ATC FWD, and ATC REV to manually command the tool changer. There are (2) tool release buttons; one on the spindle head cover and another on the keyboard.
[p84.b5] Make sure the machine is zero returned. If it is not, press POWER UP.
[p84.b6] Loading the Tool Changer
[p84.b7] CAUTION: Do not exceed the maximum tool changer specifications. Extremely heavy tool weights should be distributed evenly. This means heavy tools should be located across from one another, not next to each other. Ensure there is adequate clearance between tools in the tool changer; this distance is 3.6” for a 20-pocket and 3” for a 24+1 pocket. Check your tool changer specifications for the correct minimal clearance between tools.
[p84.b8] WARNING: Stay away from the tool changer during power up, power down, and during tool changer operations.
[p84.b9] Always load tools into the tool changer from the spindle. Never load a tool directly into the tool changer carousel. Some mills have remote tool changer controls to let you inspect and replace tools at the carousel. This station is not for initial loading and tool assignment.
[p84.b10] CAUTION: Tools that make a loud noise when released indicate a problem and should be checked before serious damage occurs to the tool changer or spindle.
[p84.b11] NOTE: Low air pressure or insufficient volume reduces the pressure applied to the tool release piston and will slow down tool change time or will not release the tool.
[p84.b12] 84 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 85 ===
[p85.b1] 9.2 | MILL - TOOL LOADING
[p85.b2] Tool Loading for a Umbrella Tool Changer
[p85.b3] 1
[p85.b4] This section tells you how to load tools into an empty tool changer for a new application. It assumes that the pocket tool table still contains information from the previous application.
[p85.b5] 1
[p85.b6] Make sure your tool holders have the correct pull stud type for the mill.
[p85.b7] 2
[p85.b8] Tools are loaded into the umbrella tool changer by first loading the tool into the spindle. To load a tool into the spindle, prepare the tool and then follow these steps:
[p85.b9] Ensure the tools loaded have the correct pull stud type for the mill.
[p85.b10] Press MDI/DNC for MDI mode.
[p85.b11] Organize the tools to match to the CNC program.
[p85.b12] Take tool in hand and insert the tool (pull stud first) into the spindle. Turn the tool so that the two cutouts in the tool holder line up with the tabs of the spindle. Push the tool upward while pressing the Tool Release button. When the tool is fitted into the spindle, release the Tool Release button.
[p85.b13] Press ATC FWD.
[p85.b14] Repeat Steps 4 and 5 with the remaining tools until all the tools are loaded.
[p85.b15] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 85
=== page 86 ===
[p86.b1] 9.3 | MILL - UMBRELLA TOOL CHANGER RECOVERY
[p86.b2] Umbrella Tool Changer Recovery
[p86.b3] If the tool changer jams, the control will automatically come to an alarm state. To correct this:
[p86.b4] WARNING: Never put your hands near the tool changer unless there is an alarm displayed first.
[p86.b5] (step 1) 1. Remove the cause of the jam.
[p86.b6] (step 2) 2. Press RESET to clear the alarms.
[p86.b7] (step 3) 3. Press RECOVER and follow the directions to reset the tool changer.
[p86.b8] 86 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual

=== page 88 ===
[p88.b1] 10.2 | MILL - TOOL TABLE
[p88.b2] Tool Table
[p88.b3] This section tells you how to use the tool table to give the control information about your tools.
[p88.b4] (step 1) 1) To access the Tool Pocket Table, press CURRENT COMMANDS and choose the Tool Table tab.
[p88.b5] (step 2) 2) Active Tool - Tells you the tool number that is installed in the spindle.
[p88.b6] (step 3) 3) Active Pocket - this show you the next pocket number.
[p88.b7] (step 4) 4) Set pocket as Large [L] - Use this flag when a large tool has a diameter of greater than 3” for 40-taper machines and greater than 4” for 50-taper machines. Scroll to the pocket of interest and press L to set the flag.
[p88.b8] CAUTION: You cannot place a large tool in the tool changer if one or both of the surrounding pockets already contain tools. Doing so causes the tool changer to crash. Large tools must have the surrounding pockets empty. However, large tools can share adjoining empty pockets.
[p88.b9] NOTE: If your machine has an umbrella-style tool changer, you will not use the tool pocket table.
[p88.b10] A Large (left), and a Heavy (not Large) Tool (above)
[p88.b11] (step 5) 5) Set pocket as heavy [H] - Use this flag when a Heavy, Small Diameter 40-taper tool (4 lb or heavier) or a 50-taper tool (12 lb or heavier) is loaded into the spindle. Scroll to the pocket of interest and press H to set the flag.
[p88.b12] (step 6) 6) Set pocket as XL [X] - Use this flag when two adjacent pockets at each side of the tool are needed. Scroll to the pocket of interest and press X to set the flag.
[p88.b13] NOTE: This option only appears if your machine is a 50-taper.
[p88.b14] (step 7) 7) Clear category [Space] - Highlight the desired tool and press SPACE to clear the flag.
[p88.b15] (step 8) 8) Set tool [###] + [Enter]- Highlight the desired pocket and type in the tool number + Enter to set the desired tool number.
[p88.b16] NOTE: You cannot assign a tool number to more than one pocket. If you enter a tool number that is already defined in the tool pocket table, you see an Invalid tool error.
[p88.b17] (step 9) 9) Clear tool [0] + [Enter]- Highlight the desired pocket and press 0 + Enter to clear out the tool number.
[p88.b18] (step 10) 10) Reset table [Origin] - Press ORIGIN with the cursor in the center column to use the ORIGIN menu. This menu lets you:
[p88.b19] Sequence All Pockets - Makes all of the tool numbers sequential based on their pocket location, starting with 1.
[p88.b20] Zero All Pockets - Removes all of the tool numbers from all of the pocket numbers.
[p88.b21] Clear Category Flags - Removes the category designations from all of the tools.
[p88.b22] (step 11) 11) * Indicates current tool changer pocket.

=== page 90 ===
[p90.b1] 10.3 | MILL - SMTC TOOL LOADING
[p90.b2] High-Speed Side-Mount Tool Changer
[p90.b3] The high-speed side-mount tool changer has an additional tool assignment, which is “Heavy”. Tools that weigh more than 4 pounds are considered heavy. You must designate heavy tools with H (Note: All large tools are considered heavy). During operation, an “h” in the tool table denotes a heavy tool in a large pocket.
[p90.b4] a large tool (must have empty pocket on both sides). The lower case “h” and “l” is placed by the control; never enter a lower case “h” or “l” into the tool table.
[p90.b5] l - Small diameter tool in a pocket reserved for a large tool in the spindle.
[p90.b6] Large tools are assumed to be heavy.
[p90.b7] As a safety precaution, the tool changer will run at a maximum of 25% of the normal speed when it changes a heavy tool. The pocket up/down speed is not slowed down. The control restores the speed to the current rapid when the tool change is complete. Contact your HFO for assistance if you have problems with unusual or extreme tooling.
[p90.b8] Heavy tools are not assumed to be large.
[p90.b9] On non-high speed tool changers, “H” and “h” have no effect.
[p90.b10] H - Heavy, but not necessarily large (large tools require empty pockets on either side).
[p90.b11] h - Heavy small diameter tool in a pocket designated for
[p90.b12] Using ‘0’ for a Tool Designation
[p90.b13] In the tool table, enter 0 (zero) for the tool number to label a tool pocket “always empty”. The tool changer does not “see” this pocket, and it never tries to install or retrieve a tool from pockets with a ‘0’ designation.
[p90.b14] You cannot use a zero to designate the tool in the spindle. The spindle must always have a tool number designation.
[p90.b15] Moving Tools in the Carousel
[p90.b16] If you need to move tools in the carousel, follow this procedure.
[p90.b17] CAUTION: Plan the reorganization of the tools in the carousel ahead of time. To reduce the potential for tool changer crashes, keep tool movement to a minimum. If there are large or heavy tools currently in the tool changer, ensure that they are only moved between tool pockets designated as such.
[p90.b18] 90 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 91 ===
[p91.b1] 10.4 | MILL - MOVING TOOLS
[p91.b2] Moving Tools in the Carousel
[p91.b3] If you need to move tools in the carousel, follow this procedure.
[p91.b4] CAUTION: Plan the reorganization of the tools in the carousel ahead of time. To reduce the potential for tool changer crashes, keep tool movement to a minimum. If there are large or heavy tools currently in the tool changer, ensure that they are only moved between tool pockets designated as such.
[p91.b5] Moving Tools
[p91.b6] The tool changer pictured has an assortment of normal- sized tools. For the purposes of this example, we need to move tool 12 to pocket 18 to make room for a large-sized tool in pocket 12.
[p91.b7] Making Room for Large Tools: [1] Tool 12 to Pocket 18, [2] Large Tool in Pocket 12.
[p91.b8] (step 1) 1) Select MDI mode. Press CURRENT COMMANDS and navigate to the TOOL TABLE display. Identify the tool number that is in pocket 12.
[p91.b9] (step 2) 2) Type Tnn (where nn is the tool number from step 1). Press ATC FWD. This places the tool from pocket 12 into the spindle.
[p91.b10] (step 3) 3) Type P18 then press ATC FWD to put the tool in the spindle into pocket 18.
[p91.b11] (step 4) 4) Scroll to pocket 12 in the TOOL TABLE and press L then ENTER to designate pocket 12 as large.
[p91.b12] (step 5) 5) Enter the tool number into SPINDLE on the TOOL TABLE. Insert the tool into the spindle.
[p91.b13] NOTE: Extra-large tools can be programmed as well. An “extra-large” tool is one that takes up three pockets; the diameter of the tool covers the tool pocket on either side of the pocket it is installed in. Contact your HFO to provide a special configuration if a tool this size is needed. The tool table must be updated since two empty pockets are needed between extra large tools.
[p91.b14] (step 6) 6) Enter P12 into the control and press ATC FWD. The tool is placed into pocket 12.
[p91.b15] 1 2
[p91.b16] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 91
=== page 92 ===
[p92.b1] 10.4 | MILL - MOVING TOOLS
[p92.b2] SMTC Tool Pre-Call
[p92.b3] Tool Pre-Call
[p92.b4] To save time, the control looks ahead as far as 80 lines into your program to process and prepare machine motion and tool changes. When look-ahead finds a tool change, the control puts the next tool in your program into position. This is called “tool pre-call.”
[p92.b5] Program commands that stop look-ahead:
[p92.b6] • Work offset selections ( G54, G55, etc.)
[p92.b7] • G103 Limit Block Buffering, when programmed without a P address or with a nonzero P address
[p92.b8] Some program commands stop look-ahead. If your program has these commands before the next tool change, the control does not pre-call the next tool. This can cause your program to run slower, because the machine must wait for the next tool to move into position before it can change tools.
[p92.b9] • M01 Optional Stop
[p92.b10] • M00 Stop Program
[p92.b11] • Block Delete Slashes ( /)
[p92.b12] • A large number of program blocks executed at high speed
[p92.b13] To make sure that the control pre-calls the next tool without look-ahead, you can command the carousel to the next tool position immediately after a tool change command, as in this code snippet:
[p92.b14] T01 M06 (TOOL CHANGE) ;
[p92.b15] T02 (PRE-CALL THE NEXT TOOL) ;
[p92.b16] 92 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 93 ===
[p93.b1] 10.5 | MILL - DOOR SWITCH PANEL
[p93.b2] SMTC Door Switch Panel
[p93.b3] Mills such as the MDC, EC-300 and EC-400 have a sub-panel to aid tool loading. The Manual/Automatic Tool Change switch must be set to “Automatic Operation” for automatic tool changer operation. If the switch is set to “Manual”, the [p93.b4] two buttons, labeled with clockwise and counterclockwise symbols, are enabled and automatic tool changes are disabled. The door has a sensor switch which detects when the door is open.
[p93.b5] Tool Changer Door Switch Panel Symbols:
[p93.b6] [1] Rotate Tool changer Carousel Counter-Clockwise,
[p93.b7] [2] Rotate Tool Changer Carousel Clockwise,
[p93.b8] 1 2
[p93.b9] [3] Tool Change Switch - Automatic Operation,
[p93.b10] [4] Tool Change Switch - Manual Operation Selection.
[p93.b11] 3 4
[p93.b12] SMTC Door Operation
[p93.b13] If the cage door is opened while a tool change is in progress, the tool change stops and resumes when the cage door is closed. Any machining operations in progress remain uninterrupted.
[p93.b14] The carousel rotates one position whenever a clockwise or counter-clockwise button is pressed once, while the switch is set to manual.
[p93.b15] During tool changer recovery, if the cage door is open or the Tool Change switch is in the manual position and RECOVER is pressed, a message is displayed telling the operator the door is open or is in manual mode. The operator must close the door and set the switch to the automatic position in order to continue.
[p93.b16] If the switch is turned to manual while a tool carousel is in motion, the tool carousel stops and resumes when the switch is turned back to automatic. The next tool change will not execute until the switch is turned back. Any machining operations that are in progress remain uninterrupted.
[p93.b17] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 93
=== page 94 ===
[p94.b1] 10.6 | MILL - SMTC RECOVERY
[p94.b2] SMTC Recovery
[p94.b3] If a problem occurred during a tool change, a tool changer recovery needs to be performed. Enter the tool changer recovery mode by:
[p94.b4] Press RECOVER and navigate to the TOOL CHANGER RECOVERY tab.
[p94.b5] Press ENTER. If there is no Alarm, the control first attempts an automatic recovery. If there is an alarm press RESET to clear the alarms and repeat from step 1.
[p94.b6] At the VMSTC TOOL RECOVERY screen, press A to begin automatic recovery or E to exit.
[p94.b7] If the automatic recovery fails, press M to continue for a manual recovery.
[p94.b8] In manual mode, follow the instructions and answer the questions to perform a proper tool changer recovery.
[p94.b9] The entire tool changer recovery process must be completed before exiting. Start the routine from the beginning if you exit the routine early.
[p94.b10] 94 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 95 ===
[p95.b1] 11.1 | MILL OPERATION - POWER ON
[p95.b2] Machine Power-On
[p95.b3] This section tells you how to power-on a new machine for the first time.
[p95.b4] • Press POWER ON until you see the Haas logo on the screen. After a self-test and boot sequence, the display shows the startup screen.
[p95.b5] The startup screen gives basic instructions to start the machine. Press CANCEL to dismiss the screen.
[p95.b6] • Turn EMERGENCY STOP to the right to reset it.
[p95.b7] • Press RESET to clear the startup alarms. If you cannot clear an alarm, the machine may need service. Contact your Haas Factory Outlet (HFO) for assistance.
[p95.b8] • If your machine is enclosed, close the doors. WARNING: Before you do the next step, remember that automatic motion begins immediately when you press POWER UP. Make sure the motion path is clear. Stay away from the spindle, machine table, and tool changer.
[p95.b9] Spindle Warm-Up
[p95.b10] If your machine’s spindle has been idle for more than (4) days, run the spindle warm-up program before you use the machine. This program brings the spindle up to speed slowly to distribute the lubrication and let the spindle reach a stable temperature.
[p95.b11] Your machine includes a 20-minute warm-up program (O09220) in the program list. If you use the spindle at consistent high speeds, you should run this program every day.
[p95.b12] • Press POWER UP. After the first POWER UP, the axes move toward their home positions. The axes then move slowly until the machine finds the home switch for each axis. This establishes the machine home position.
[p95.b13] Press any of the following:
[p95.b14] • CANCEL to dismiss the screen.
[p95.b15] • CYCLE START to run current program.
[p95.b16] • HANDLE JOG for manual operation.
[p95.b17] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 95
=== page 96 ===
[p96.b1] 11.2 | MILL OPERATION - SCREEN CAPTURE
[p96.b2] Screen Capture
[p96.b3] The control can capture and save an image of the current screen to an attached USB device or the User Data memory.
[p96.b4] Result:
[p96.b5] The control saves the screen capture to your USB device or control memory. The message Snapshot saved to USB or Snapshot saved to User Data appears when the process finishes.
[p96.b6] Enter a filename if desired. If no filename is entered, the system will use the default filename (see note).
[p96.b7] Press SHIFT.
[p96.b8] Press F1.
[p96.b9] NOTE: The control uses the default filename snapshot#. png. The # starts from 0 and increments each time you capture a screen. This counter resets at power off. Screen captures that you take after a power cycle overwrite previous screen captures that have the same filename on the User Data memory.
[p96.b10] Error Report
[p96.b11] The control can generate an error report that saves the state of the machine that is used for analysis. This is useful when helping the HFO troubleshoot an intermittent problem.
[p96.b12] Result:
[p96.b13] The control saves the error report to your USB device or control memory. The error report is a zip file that includes a screen capture, the active program, and other information used for diagnostics. Generate this error report when an error or an alarm occurs. E-mail the error report to your local Haas Factory Outlet.
[p96.b14] (step 1) 1. Press SHIFT.
[p96.b15] (step 2) 2. Press F3.
[p96.b16] NOTE: Be sure to always generate the error report with the alarm or the error is active.
[p96.b17] 96 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual

[p97.b1] 11.3 | MILL OPERATION - PROGRAM SEARCH
[p97.b2] Basic Program Search
[p97.b3] You can use this function to quickly find code in a program.
[p97.b4] NOTE: This is a quick-search function that finds the first match in the search direction that you specify. You can use the Editor for a more full-featured search. Refer to Chapter 6.5 for more information on the Editor search function.
[p97.b5] NOTE: This is a quick-search function that finds the first match in the search direction that you specify. You can use the Editor for a more full-featured search. Refer to The Search Menu for more information on the Editor search function.
[p97.b6] Type the text you want to find in the active program.
[p97.b7] Press the UP or DOWN cursor arrow key.
[p97.b8] Locate the Last Program Error
[p97.b9] Starting in software version 100.19.000.1100 the control can find the last error in a program.
[p97.b10] Press SHIFT + F4 to display the last line of G-code that generated the error.
[p97.b11] Result:
[p97.b12] The UP cursor arrow key searches from the cursor position to the start of the program. The DOWN cursor arrow key searches to the end of the program. The control highlights the first match.
[p97.b13] NOTE: Putting your search term within parentheses (), will search only within comment lines.

=== page 98 ===
[p98.b1] 11.4 | MILL OPERATION - SAFE RUN MODE
[p98.b2] Safe Run Mode
[p98.b3] The purpose of Safe Run is to reduce damage to the machine in the event of a crash. It does not prevent crashes, but it raises an alarm sooner and backs off from the crash location.
[p98.b4] Safe Run does the following:
[p98.b5] • Slow down the speed of the motion.
[p98.b6] • Increases the position error sensitivity.
[p98.b7] NOTE: The Safe Run feature is available starting in software version 100.19.000.1300.
[p98.b8] • When a crash is detected, the control will immediately reverse the axis by a small amount. This will prevent the motor from continuing to drive into the object it has crashed into as well as relieve pressure from the crash itself. After Safe Run has detected a crash, you should be able to easily fit a piece off paper between the two surfaces that crashed.
[p98.b9] Safe Run Supported Machines
[p98.b10] • VF-1 through VF-5
[p98.b11] • VM-2/3
[p98.b12] NOTE: Safe Run is intended for running a program for the first time after writing or changing it. Is it not recommended to run a reliable program with Safe Run, as it increases cycle time significantly. The tool may break and the work piece may still be damaged in a crash.
[p98.b13] • UMC-500/750/1000
[p98.b14] • All DM’s
[p98.b15] • All DT’s
[p98.b16] • All TM’s
[p98.b17] • ST-10 through ST-35
[p98.b18] Common causes for crashes are:
[p98.b19] Incorrect tool offsets.
[p98.b20] Incorrect work offsets.
[p98.b21] Wrong tool in the spindle.
[p98.b22] NOTE: The Safe Run feature will only detect a crash in handle jog and rapid (G00), it will not detect a crash in a feed move.
[p98.b23] 98 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 99 ===
[p99.b1] 11.4 | MILL OPERATION - SAFE RUN MODE
[p99.b2] Safe Run is active during jogging as well. Safe Run can be used during job setup to protect against accidental crashes due to operator error.
[p99.b3] If your machine supports Safe Run, you will see a new icon in MDI with the text F3 Activate Safe Run [1]. Press F3 to turn Safe Run on/off. Safe Run Active state is noted by a water mark [2] in the program panel.
[p99.b4] It is only active during rapid motions. Rapid motions include G00, Home G28, moving to tool changes, and the non-machining motions of canned cycles. Any machining motion such as a feed or tap will not have safe mode active.
[p99.b5] Safe Run is not active during feeds due to the nature of crash detection. Cutting forces cannot be discerned from crashes.
[p99.b6] When a crash is detected, all motion is brought to a stop, an alarm [1] is generated, and a popup [2] is generated letting the operator know that a crash was detected, and which axis it was detected on. This alarm can be cleared by reset.
[p99.b7] In certain cases the pressure against the part may not have been relieved by the Safe Run back-off. In the worse case, an additional crash may be generated after you have reset the alarm. If this happens, turn Safe Run off and jog the axis away from the crash location.
[p99.b8] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 99
=== page 100 ===
[p100.b1] 11.5 | MILL OPERATION - RUN-STOP-JOG-CONTINUE
[p100.b2] Run-Stop-Jog-Continue
[p100.b3] This feature lets you stop a running program, jog away from the part, and then start the program again.
[p100.b4] (step 6) 6. Press CYCLE START. The control rapids X, Y, and rotary axes at 5% to the position where you pressed FEED HOLD. It then returns the Z Axis. If you press FEED HOLD during this motion, axis motion pauses and the control gives the message Jog Return Hold. Press CYCLE START to resume the Jog Return motion. The control goes into a feed hold state again when the motion is finished. CAUTION: The control does not follow the same path that you used to jog away.
[p100.b5] (step 1) 1. Press FEED HOLD. Axis motion stops. The spindle continues to turn.
[p100.b6] (step 2) 2. Press X, Y, Z, or an installed Rotary Axis (A for A Axis, B for B Axis, and C for C Axis), then press HANDLE JOG. The control stores the current X, Y, Z, and rotary axes positions.
[p100.b7] (step 3) 3. The control gives the message Jog Away and displays the Jog Away icon. Use the jog handle or jog keys to move the tool away from the part. You can start or stop the spindle with FWD, REV, or STOP. You can command optional Through Spindle Coolant on and off with the AUX CLNT key (you must stop the spindle first). Command optional Through Tool Air Blast on and off with SHIFT + AUX CLNT keys. Command Coolant on and off with the COOLANT key. Command the Auto Air Gun / Minimum Quantity Lubrication options with SHIFT + COOLANT keys. You can also release the tool to change inserts.
[p100.b8] (step 7) 7. Press CYCLE START again and the program resumes operation.
[p100.b9] CAUTION: If Setting 36 is ON, the control scans the program to make sure the machine is in the correct state (tools, offsets, G- and M-codes, etc.) to safely continue the program. If Setting 36 is OFF, the control does not scan the program. This can save time, but it could cause a crash in an unproven program.
[p100.b10] CAUTION: When you start the program again, the control uses the previous offsets for the return position. Therefore, it is unsafe and not recommended to change tools and offsets when you interrupt a program.
[p100.b11] (step 4) 4. Jog to a position as close as possible to the stored position, or to a position where there is an unobstructed rapid path back to the stored position.
[p100.b12] (step 5) 5. Press MEMORY or MDI to return to run mode. The control gives the message Jog Return and displays the Jog Return icon. The control continues only if you return to the mode that was in effect when you stopped the program.
[p100.b13] 100 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual
=== page 101 ===
[p101.b1] 11.6 | MILL OPERATION - GRAPHICS MODE
[p101.b2] Graphics Mode
[p101.b3] A safe way to troubleshoot a program is to press GRAPHICS to run it in graphics mode. No movement occurs on the machine, instead the movement is illustrated on the screen.
[p101.b4] (step 1) 1) Axis Planes Press 1 to view the graphics in G17 plane, press 2 forG18 plane or press 3 to view in G19 plane.
[p101.b5] (step 2) 2) Key Help Area The lower-left part of the graphics display pane is the function key help area. This area shows you the function keys that you can use, and a description of what they do.
[p101.b6] (step 3) 3) Locator Window The lower-right part of the pane displays the simulated machine table area, and it shows where the simulated view is zoomed and focused.
[p101.b7] (step 4) 4) Graphics Speed Press f3 or f4 to run the desired graphics speed.
[p101.b8] (step 5) 5) Tool Path Window The large window in the center of the display gives a simulated view of the work area. It displays a cutting tool icon and simulated tool paths.
[p101.b9] NOTE: Feed motion appears as a black line. Rapid moves appear as a green line. Drill cycle locations appear with an X.
[p101.b10] NOTE: If Setting 253 is ON, the tool diameter is shown as a thin line. If it is OFF, the tool diameter specified in the Tool Offsets Diameter Geometry table is used.
[p101.b11] (step 6) 6) Zoom Press F2 to display a rectangle (zoom window) that shows the area that the zoom operation will move to. Use PAGE DOWN to decrease the size of the zoom window (zoom in), and use PAGE UP to increase the size of the zoom window (zoom out). Use the cursor arrow keys to move the zoom window to the location you want to zoom, and press ENTER to complete the zoom. The control scales the tool path window to the zoom window. Run the program again to display the tool path. Press F2 and then HOME to expand the Tool Path window to cover the entire work area.
[p101.b12] (step 7) 7) Z-Axis Part Zero Line The horizontal line on the Z-Axis bar at the top-right corner of the graphics screen gives the position of the current Z-axis work offset plus the length of the current tool. While a program simulation runs, the shaded portion of the bar indicates the depth of the simulated Z-Axis motion relative to the Z-Axis work zero position.
[p101.b13] (step 8) 8) Position Pane The position pane displays axis locations just as it would during a live part run.
[p101.b14] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 101
=== page 102 ===
[p102.b1] 12.1 | MILL - PROGRAMMING
[p102.b2] Basic Programming
[p102.b3] A typical CNC program has (3) parts:
[p102.b4] (step 1) 1) Preparation: This portion of the program selects the work and tool offsets, selects the cutting tool, turns on the coolant, sets spindle speed, and selects absolute or incremental positioning for axis motion.
[p102.b5] O40001 (Basic program) ;
[p102.b6] (G54 X0 Y0 is top right corner of part) ;
[p102.b7] (Z0 is on top of the part) ;
[p102.b8] (step 2) 2) Cutting: This portion of the program defines the tool path and feed rate for the cutting operation.
[p102.b9] (T1 is a 1/2” end mill) ;
[p102.b10] (BEGIN PREPARATION BLOCKS) ;
[p102.b11] (step 3) 3) Completion: This portion of the program moves the spindle out of the way, turns off the spindle, turns off the coolant, and moves the table to a position from where the part can be unloaded and inspected.
[p102.b12] T1 M06 (Select tool 1) ;
[p102.b13] G00 G90 G17 G40 G49 G54 (Safe startup) ;
[p102.b14] X0 Y0 (Rapid to 1st position) ;
[p102.b15] This is a basic program that makes a 0.100” (2.54 mm) deep cut with Tool 1 in a piece of material along a straight line path from X = 0.0, Y = 0.0 to X = - 4.0, Y = - 4.0.
[p102.b16] S1000 M03 (Spindle on CW) ;
[p102.b17] G43 H01 Z0.1 (Tool offset 1 on) ;
[p102.b18] NOTE: A program block can contain more than one G-code, as long as those G-codes are from different groups. You cannot place two G-codes from the same group in a program block. Also note that only one M-code per block is allowed.
[p102.b19] M08 (Coolant on) ;
[p102.b20] (BEGIN CUTTING BLOCKS) ;
[p102.b21] G01 F20. Z-0.1 (Feed to cutting depth) ;
[p102.b22] X-4. Y-4. (linear motion) ;
[p102.b23] (BEGIN COMPLETION BLOCKS) ;
[p102.b24] G00 Z0.1 M09 (Rapid retract, Coolant off) ;
[p102.b25] G53 G49 Z0 M05 (Z home, Spindle off) ;
[p102.b26] G53 Y0 (Y home) ;
[p102.b27] M30 (End program) ;
[p102.b28] 102 | 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual

[p103.b1] 12.1 | MILL - PROGRAMMING
[p103.b2] Preparation
[p103.b3] These are the preparation code blocks in the sample program O40001:
[p103.t1.r1] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: PREPARATION CODE BLOCK | DESCRIPTION: DESCRIPTION
[p103.t1.r2] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: % | DESCRIPTION: Denotes the beginning of a program written in a text editor.
[p103.t1.r3] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: O40001 (Basic program) ; | DESCRIPTION: O40001 is the name of the program. Program naming convention follows the Onnnnn format: The letter “O”, or “o” is followed by a 5-digit number.
[p103.t1.r4] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: (G54 X0 Y0 is top right corner of part) ; | DESCRIPTION: Comment
[p103.t1.r5] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: (Z0 is on top of the part) ; | DESCRIPTION: Comment
[p103.t1.r6] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: (T1 is a 1/2" end mill) ; | DESCRIPTION: Comment
[p103.t1.r7] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: (BEGIN PREPARATION BLOCKS) ; | DESCRIPTION: Comment
[p103.t1.r8] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: T1 M06 (Select tool 1) ; | DESCRIPTION: Selects tool T1 to be used. M06 commands the tool changer to load Tool 1 (T1) into the spindle.
[p103.t1.r9] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: G00 G90 G17 G40 G49 G54 (Safe startup) ; | DESCRIPTION: This is referred to as a safe startup line. It is good machining practice to place this block of code after every tool change. G00 defines axis movement following it to be completed in Rapid Motion mode. G90 defines axis movements that will be completed in absolute mode (refer to page Absolute vs. Incremental Positioning (G90, G91) for more information). G17 defines the cutting plane as the XY plane. G40 cancels Cutter Compensation. G49 cancels tool length compensation. G54 defines the coordinate system to be centered on the Work Offset stored in G54 on the Offset display.
[p103.b4] DESCRIPTION PREPARATION
[p103.b5] 2023 Mill Operator’s Manual | 103

[p104.b1] 12.1 | MILL - PROGRAMMING
[p104.b2] Preparation (Contin.)
[p104.t1.r1] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: PREPARATION CODE BLOCK | DESCRIPTION: DESCRIPTION
[p104.t1.r2] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: X0 Y0 (Rapid to 1st position) ; | DESCRIPTION: X0 Y0 commands the table to move to the position X = 0.0 and Y = 0.0 in the G54 coordinate system.
[p104.t1.r3] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: S1000 M03 (Spindle on CW) ; | DESCRIPTION: M03 turns the spindle on in a clockwise direction. It takes the address code Snnnn, where nnnn is the desired spindle RPM. On machines with a gearbox, the control automatically selects high gear or low gear, based on the commanded spindle speed. You can use an M41 or M42 to override this. Refer to page M41 Low Gear Override / M42 High Gear Override for more information on these M-codes. On machines with a gearbox, the control automatically selects high gear or low gear, based on the commanded spindle speed. You can use an M41 or M42 to override this. Refer to M41 / M42 Low / High Gear Override for more information on these M-codes.
[p104.t1.r4] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: G43 H01 Z0.1 (Tool offset 1 on) ; | DESCRIPTION: G43 H01 turns on Tool Length Compensation +. The H01 specifies to use the length stored for Tool 1 in the Tool Offset display. Z0.1 commands the Z Axis to Z=0.1.
[p104.t1.r5] PREPARATION
CODE BLOCK: M08 (Coolant on) ; | DESCRIPTION: M08commands the coolant to turn on.
[p104.b3] Cutting
[p104.b4] These are the preparation code blocks in the sample program O40001:
[p104.t2.r1] TABLE COLUMNS: CUTTING CODE BLOCK | DESCRIPTION
[p104.t2.r2] CUTTING CODE BLOCK: G01 F20. Z-0.1 (Feed to cutting depth) ; | DESCRIPTION: G01 F20. defines axis movements after it to be completed in a straight line. G01 requires the address code Fnnn.nnnn. The address code F20. specifies that the feed rate for the motion is 20” (508 mm) / min. Z-0.1 commands the Z Axis to Z = - 0.1.
[p104.t2.r3] CUTTING CODE BLOCK: X-4. Y-4. (linear motion) ; | DESCRIPTION: X-4. Y-4. commands the X Axis to move to X = - 4.0 and commands the Y Axis to move to Y = - 4.0.
